> cycle README's 0.1.5 row owes; the harness's text-mode reads of source that

>

> new `[toolchain]` manifest rows, `Pod` → the prelude `Copy`, `cstring`
> becoming owning (the author's decision in the compiler seat, its census still

> what cycle 0.2's adoption owes, measured where it can be.
>
> **MEASURED FIRST** (§1), at compiler `c970483`, and at `c3bdae2` wherever a
> line says so. The unchanged tree is `GREEN -- 112 unit(s)` — 0.1.3c's,
> verified PASS. **The close adds no test and removes none: 112 stays 112.**
> What moves is the harness and the documents: `harness/lexical.py` joins; the
> self-check plants 40 tree-check violations where it planted 23, drives
> `run._verdict` on four specimens where it drove three, and gains a fifth part
> that asks the reader and the pinned compiler about one text of every lexical

>
> **Found at planning** — each is a decision, a correction, or a hand-on:
>
> 1. **The text-mode reads were one shape of eight** (§1.3). The compiler's
>    lexer ends a `//` comment at byte 10 alone, closes a `/* */` at its first
>    `*/`, reads `'"'` as one character and a template's text as text, and

>    nothing else, and its import walk read the literal's text. Eight shapes,
>    each planted through the check it could fool: **six passed a violation the
>    compiler compiles**, and two fired on text the compiler reads as a
>    comment. And `newline=""` alone — 0.1.4c's suggested fix — leaves the
>    carriage return to `splitlines()`, which splits at it anyway (§1.5).
>    **PD-50 ports `nitpick-regex`'s reader.**
> 2. **Every check matched its pattern against one line** (§1.4), and the

>    eleven shapes, each a call, an index or a declaration to the compiler, each
>    passed by one of five checks; and a `main` split the same way was `none` to
>    `run._verdict`. **PD-51.**
> 3. **Cycle 0.0 deferred two checks to this cycle and this cycle built neither**

>    **PD-52 re-homes both**, with the measurement that the length is guarded by

## Why 0.1.5, and why its shape

**The letter was given with the cycle**: the README's last row is the close,
and the row has grown a list of what earlier subcycles found and did not own.
W-22 gives the close two halves, and the worker skill's §7 and §8 give each
its checklist: the first reports `READY-TO-CLOSE` with `AUDIT: none`; the second
works with the audit in hand.

- *Declined — the harness's reader as a subcycle of its own before the close:*
  the close carries the item by its README row; cycle 0.0.6's close made eight
  instrument repairs; and the cycle audit that follows reads the new reader as
  it reads everything else, which is the review a reader port wants. §2 says
  why the full extent, and not the one shape the row named, is the item.
- *Declined — the close archiving in its first half:* a folder in `done/` is a
  claim that the cycle is finished, and `nitpick-regex` archived at its first
  close attempt and had to reverse it when the audit refused (its RX-140).
  The archive is the second half's, after the audit.
- *Declined — the close taking the re-pin:* the author's decision puts it at
  the end of the compiler's 1.6.1d, one round, and it is cycle 0.2's adoption.

**The variables.** A Bash call keeps no variables, so they live in a file.
Once, with `REPO` set to the dispatch's absolute `REPO`:

```bash
mkdir -p "$REPO/.internal/w015"
cat > "$REPO/.internal/w015/env.sh" <<'EOF2'
export REPO=$(realpath "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../..")
export WB=$(realpath "$REPO/..")
export NPK_TREE=$(realpath "$WB/../nitpick")
export PIN=c970483
export OLD=c3bdae2
export NPKC=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN/npkc
export NPKRT=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN/npkrt.o
export W=$REPO/.internal/w015
export CHECK=$WB/skills/check/scripts
export PLAN=$REPO/meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md
EOF2
```

— and then **at the top of every shell**, with the dispatch's absolute `REPO`
written out: `source <REPO>/.internal/w015/env.sh`. `$W` is gitignored scratch
(`.internal`, the harness's `WALK_SKIP`); every script this plan uses lives
there and none is committed — **its text is committed, here**. The harness
reads `NPKC` and `NPKRT` from the environment, so every harness run below is at
`c970483`. **Every workbench script runs under `python3 -B`**, because an
import there writes `__pycache__` into a tree this subcycle may not write; the
plan's own scripts do too.

**The text is applied from this file, not typed from it.** Every block the
worker writes into the tree is a fenced block below headed by a marker line —
`apply GROUP: new PATH` for a new file, `apply GROUP: old PATH` then `apply
GROUP: to PATH` for a replacement, in the groups `code` (§4), `docs` and
`decisions` (§5) — and `script: NAME` for a script; `apply.py` applies one
group in document order, each old text asserted to occur EXACTLY ONCE, stopping
at the first that does not, and `apply.py check GROUP` reports what a group
would do and writes nothing. **A tag is spelled `{{sweep: name=N}}` in this
file** and `apply.py` writes it `[[sweep: name=N]]` into the tree. **A link in a
block is spelled with an `@` between its closing bracket and its opening
parenthesis** — `check_refs` resolves a link inside this file's fences from
THIS file's directory, where a link quoted from another file would not
resolve — and `apply.py` and `same.py` read it back as the link it is.
And **a decision this subcycle adds is written `PD-50` … `PD-52` outside
`DECISIONS.md` and `TM-<PD-50>` inside it**, which `renumber.py` (step 3) turns
into the numbers. Extract `apply.py` itself, then every script:

```bash
python3 -B - "$PLAN" "$W" <<'EOF2'
import re, sys
text = open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8").read()
m = re.search(r"^<!-- script: apply\.py -->\n(`{3,})[a-z]*\n(.*?)\n\1\n", text, re.S | re.M)
open(sys.argv[2] + "/apply.py", "w", encoding="utf-8").write(m.group(2) + "\n")
print("apply.py:", m.group(2).count("\n") + 1, "lines")
EOF2
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/apply.py" scripts
```

Expected: `apply.py: 106 lines`, then twelve `script` lines in document
order — `apply.py`, `same.py` (§0), `renumber.py` (§5.8), `stale.py`,
`reasons.py` (§6), `lexsame.py`, `shapes.py`, `facts.py`, `sweep.py` (§7 step
1), `mut.py` (step 5), `ci_read.py` (step 7) and `archive.py` (§8.4) — and
`scripts written: 12`.

<!-- script: apply.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5's text, applied from THIS PLAN rather than transcribed from it
(`0.1.5.md` §0) -- 0.1.3c's `apply.py`, with this plan's name and one
addition, the spelling of a link below. It reads and writes every file with
`newline=""`, so a byte the plan does not name is never translated (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §6: read source the way the compiler reads it); and
`check GROUP` reports what a group would do and writes nothing.
Every block the worker applies is a fenced block in the plan headed by one
marker line:

    <!-- apply GROUP: new PATH -->   the block is a new file; PATH must not exist
    <!-- apply GROUP: old PATH -->   the block must occur EXACTLY ONCE in PATH ...
    <!-- apply GROUP: to PATH -->    ... and the next block replaces it
    <!-- script: NAME -->            the block is a script, written to $W/NAME

A TAG IS SPELLED `{{sweep: name=N}}` in the plan and written `[[sweep: name=N]]`,
and A LINK'S CLOSING BRACKET AND OPENING PARENTHESIS ARE SPELLED WITH AN `@`
BETWEEN THEM in the plan and written together: a roadmap file's whole tag is a
claim `check_denominators` holds the tree to, and `check_refs` resolves a
markdown link inside the plan's fences from the PLAN's directory, where a link
quoted from another file would not resolve.

`python3 apply.py GROUP` applies one group's blocks in document order and
stops at the first that does not apply, having written nothing for it;
`python3 apply.py check GROUP` does the same in memory and writes nothing;
`python3 apply.py scripts` writes every script. So what runs is the plan's
own text, byte for byte."""
import os, re, sys

REPO, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"]
PLAN = os.environ.get("PLAN", os.path.join(REPO, "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md"))
FENCE = r"\n(?P<fence>`{3,})[a-z]*\n(?P<body>.*?)\n(?P=fence)\n"
EDIT = re.compile(r"^<!-- apply (\w+): (new|old|to) (\S+) -->" + FENCE, re.S | re.M)
SCRIPT = re.compile(r"^<!-- script: (\S+) -->" + FENCE, re.S | re.M)
LINK_AT, LINK = "]" + "@" + "(", "]" + "("
TAG = re.compile(r"\{\{(sweep:\s*[a-z_]+\s*=\s*-?\d+)\}\}")


def read(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def write(path, text):
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)


text = read(PLAN)
args = sys.argv[1:]
dry = bool(args) and args[0] == "check"
if dry:
    args = args[1:]
if len(args) != 1:
    sys.exit("usage: apply.py [check] GROUP | apply.py scripts")
want = args[0]
if want == "scripts":
    done = 0
    for m in SCRIPT.finditer(text):
        body = m.group("body") + "\n"
        write(os.path.join(W, m.group(1)), body)
        print("  script %-26s %4d lines" % (m.group(1), body.count("\n")))
        done += 1
    print("scripts written: %d" % done)
    sys.exit(0 if done else 1)
pending, done, memory = None, 0, {}
for m in EDIT.finditer(text):
    grp, kind, path = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
    body = TAG.sub(r"[[\1]]", m.group("body") + "\n").replace(LINK_AT, LINK)
    if grp != want:
        continue
    full = os.path.join(REPO, path)
    if kind == "new":
        if os.path.exists(full) or full in memory:
            sys.exit("STOP: %s already exists; `new` writes only a file that does not" % path)
        if dry:
            memory[full] = body
        else:
            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(full), exist_ok=True)
            write(full, body)
        print("  new    %-58s %4d lines" % (path, body.count("\n")))
        done += 1
    elif kind == "old":
        if pending is not None:
            sys.exit("STOP: an `old %s` with no `to` after it" % pending[0])
        pending = (path, body)
    else:
        if pending is None or pending[0] != path:
            sys.exit("STOP: a `to %s` with no `old` for the same file before it" % path)
        cur = memory[full] if full in memory else read(full)
        n = cur.count(pending[1])
        if n != 1:
            sys.exit("STOP: in %s the old text occurs %d times, not once:\n%s"
                     % (path, n, pending[1][:300]))
        new = cur.replace(pending[1], body)
        if dry:
            memory[full] = new
        else:
            write(full, new)
        print("  edit   %-58s -%d +%d lines" % (path, pending[1].count("\n"), body.count("\n")))
        pending = None
        done += 1
if pending is not None:
    sys.exit("STOP: an `old %s` with no `to` after it" % pending[0])
print("group %s: %d block(s) %s" % (want, done, "would apply" if dry else "applied"))
sys.exit(0 if done else 1)
````

`same.py` compares a saved output with a block this plan expects, line for
line, with each wall-clock figure (`N.N s`) set aside — 0.1.3c's, pointed at
this file. **A command's whole output is compared, never a reading of it** (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §12: care drops lines; a comparator does not).

<!-- script: same.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5: a saved output against the block this plan expects, line for line.

    same.py NAME FILE

reads the fenced block after the marker line `<!-- expect: NAME -->` in the
plan and the lines of FILE, sets every wall-clock figure (`12.3 s`) aside in
both, and prints each line that differs, both ways, then `NAME: N line(s)
expected, D difference(s)`. Exit 0 only at 0 -- so the comparison is the
script's and not a reading. A tag in a block is spelled `{{sweep: name=N}}`,
and a link with an `@` between its closing bracket and its opening
parenthesis, as `apply.py`'s docstring says why; each is read back as the
output writes it."""
import os, re, sys

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
PLAN = os.environ.get("PLAN", os.path.join(REPO, "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md"))
name, path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
text = open(PLAN, encoding="utf-8").read()
m = re.search(r"^<!-- expect: %s -->\n(`{3,})[a-z]*\n(.*?)\n\1\n" % re.escape(name),
              text, re.S | re.M)
if not m:
    sys.exit("STOP: the plan has no expected block named %r" % name)
CLOCK = re.compile(r"\b\d+\.\d+ s\b")


def lines(s):
    return [CLOCK.sub("N s", l.rstrip()) for l in s.splitlines()]


TAG = re.compile(r"\{\{(sweep:\s*[a-z_]+\s*=\s*-?\d+)\}\}")
want = lines(TAG.sub(r"[[\1]]", m.group(2)).replace("]" + "@" + "(", "]" + "("))
got = lines(open(path, encoding="utf-8").read())
diffs = 0
for i in range(max(len(want), len(got))):
    w = want[i] if i < len(want) else "(no line)"
    g = got[i] if i < len(got) else "(no line)"
    if w != g:
        diffs += 1
        print("  line %d\n    plan: %s\n    run:  %s" % (i + 1, w, g))
print("%s: %d line(s) expected, %d difference(s)" % (name, len(want), diffs))
sys.exit(0 if diffs == 0 else 1)
````

**Every removal in this plan names its target through `"${W:?}"`** or
`"${REPO:?}"`, never a bare variable: an unset one would turn `rm -rf "$W/…"`
into a removal at the filesystem's root, and this environment's safety check
refuses the bare form.

## 1. What was measured — the facts this plan rests on

All at `c970483` unless a line says `c3bdae2`; LLVM 20.1.2; every program
through `npkc`, `llc`, `ld.lld` and a run, at -O0 and again through `opt -O2`,
the same on both legs unless a line says otherwise. **Step 1's `facts.py`
re-derives every line of this section before anything is changed** — it
applies this plan's own `code` group to a copy of the tree exported by
`git archive`, so what it measures is the planned text, and it writes nothing
in the tree — and its whole output is §7's `facts` block.

### 1.1 The pin

`npkc` 9 751 776 bytes, `e4d95007…`, and `npkrt.o` 72 576 bytes, `162b8975…`,
the anchor, the same object at `c3bdae2` and `c970483`. The compiler tree at
`c970483` is read with `git show` and never through its working tree.

### 1.2 The unchanged tree, and what the close moves

`GREEN -- 112 unit(s), 0 failures; 5 pending` at `c970483`, about 131 s — the
tree 0.1.3c left, verified PASS, with this plan committed on it. **With every
group of this plan applied it is `GREEN -- 112` still**, and the run's output
differs from the unchanged tree's in exactly these lines, measured (§7 step
5's `rundiff`):

| The line | Unchanged | Applied |
|---|---|---|
| the self-check's header | `… then 23 tree-check violations,` | `… then 40 tree-check violations,` |
| the tree checks | `23 planted violation(s) caught, 23 clean control(s) silent` | `40 …, 40 …` |
| the verdict mechanisms | `3 specimen(s) for _verdict; …` | `4 specimen(s) …` |
| the reader | — | `ok    the reader (lexical.py)    E1: 23 lines of every form read back, 10 import(s) as the compiler reads them; E2: the pinned compiler runs every observable form to 0` |
| `check_denominators` | `55 tagged number(s) over 238 file(s)` | `… over 239 file(s)` — `lexical.py` is one more `.py` it reads |
| `check_error_budget`'s report | `… -- expected: no module raises anything before cycle 0.1.` | `… -- expected: each arrives with the module SAFETY.md S-4's table names for it.` |
| `check_specs_current` | `… over 218 file(s); 334 declared rule(s) …; 2 unresolved`, and two reports, V-1k and V-1l cited — this plan cites the two rules its `docs` group declares | `… over 219 file(s); 339 declared rule(s) …; 0 unresolved` — V-1k, V-1l and the three new decisions |

**No unit's verdict moves, and no `.npk` file's**: the close edits the comments
of eleven `.npk` files and nothing else in them, each edit to a refused file
line for line, so no position the compiler reports moves
(`missing_failsafe/case1` asserts its, `62:1`), and step 5's per-file sweep of
all 127 files at the pin says so — and reads every file's code, comments and
literals blanked, against `HEAD`'s. **No arm bill moves** — `cal` 11, the
umbrella 13, the rest 6 — and the library's IR is 228 227 bytes before and
after: `src/` changes in two comments of `src/core/core.npk`, line for line.

### 1.3 The reader: eight shapes, and the compiler's reading of each

**The compiler's lexer, read at `c970483`** (`src/frontend/lexer.npk`):
`is_space` is space, tab, CR and LF; `lexer_advance` counts a line at byte 10
alone; `lexer_skip_trivia` ends a `//` comment at byte 10 — a CR before it is
comment text — and closes a `/* */` at its first `*/`, not nesting (*"nesting
makes an unterminated comment swallow the rest of the file silently"*);
`lexer_next` reads `'x'` as one code point, a template's text as text, a raw
string without escapes, a block string to its first `"""` (the compiler's
1.6.0 step 3e, DEF-98), and ends a plain string at a newline; and
`p_parse_import` takes a `use` path as its literal's DECODED value
(`escapes.npk`). **Measured, not only read** — each shape as a program at
`c970483` and at `c3bdae2`, the same verdict at both (and at the workbench's
control copy `9f6f370`, at planning):

| Program | The compiler | What it shows |
|---|---|---|
| `a = 1;` then `// note` CR `a = 2;` on one line | runs, exits 1 | a lone CR does not end a `//` comment |
| `/* x // y */ a = 3;` | exits 3 | a `//` inside `/* */` is inert, and the code after the block runs |
| `/* p /* q */ a = 4;` | exits 4 | a `/* */` does not nest |
| `char8:q = '"'; a = 5;` | exits 5 | `'"'` is one character |
| ``string:t = `x//y`; a = 6;`` | exits 6 | a template's `//` is text |
| `use ".\x2fsub/lx_six.npk".*;` and `raw six()` | exits 6 | a `use` path is its decoded value |
| `// note` CR `use "./sub/lx_six.npk".*;` | exits 7; calling `six()` is `NITPICK-RESOLVE-002` | a `use` after a lone CR in a comment is comment text |

**This harness's reading, before this plan**: every `.npk` read in Python's
text mode at seven sites, which turns a lone CR into a line end before anything
scans the text; comments blanked by `checks.strip_comments`, which knows `//`
and the double-quoted string and nothing else; strings blanked by
`checks.strip_strings`, which knows `"` and runs a string across newlines; and
imports read by `build.imports_of`, one regular expression per line over the
literal's TEXT. **Each shape planted in a scratch tree built by the harness's
own `selfcheck._mini_tree`, through the check it could fool** (`shapes.py`,
part C of `facts.py`):

| Row | Shape | Check | Right answer | HEAD's harness |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | `// note` CR `"`, then `y / m` on the next line | `check_literal_divisors` | red | **silent** — the CR ended the comment, the `"` opened a string that blanked the division |
| P2 | `/* see // below */ pass mono_now();` | `check_purity` | red | **silent** — the `//` blanked the rest of the line |
| P3 | `/* a // note */ pub error:ETimeOops;` | `check_error_budget` | red | **silent** — a fourth identity, unseen |
| P4 | `char8:q = '"'; pass y / m;` | `check_literal_divisors` | red | **silent** — the `"` opened a string to the end of the file |
| P5 | ``string:t = `a//b`; pass y / m;`` | `check_literal_divisors` | red | **silent** — the template's `//` blanked the line |
| P6 | `use "..\x2fhost/host.npk".*;` in `cal` | `check_layering` | red | **silent** — a directory named `..\x2fhost`, in `cal`'s own layer |
| P7 | `// note` CR `use "../host/host.npk".*;` in `cal` | `check_layering` | silent | **RED** — a comment read as an import of `host` |
| P8 | a `pub use` inside a `/* */`, the re-export count tagged 1 | `check_denominators` | silent | **RED** — the count took every LINE beginning `pub use ` |

**Eight of eight rows disagree with the compiler at HEAD, and none with this
plan's reader** (`facts.py` part C prints both columns for all nineteen rows of
§1.3 and §1.4). The six silent rows are violations the compiler compiles; the
two red rows fire on text the compiler reads as a comment.
`check_literal_divisors`' own docstring named block comments, character
literals and templates as its limits and called the gap *"the safe
direction"* — a `/` in one would read as a division and fail loud; P4 and P5
measure the opposite, a division hidden.

### 1.4 The tokens: eleven shapes, and every check matched a line

**PD-50's reader makes the harness see the file the compiler sees. It does not
make a check see the tokens the compiler sees**: every tree check but
`check_literal_divisors` matched its pattern against one line at a time,
several of them anchored at the line's start, and the compiler reads tokens with its
whitespace — a newline included — between them. **Measured at `c970483` and
`c3bdae2`**, each shape as a program:

| Program | The compiler |
|---|---|
| `raw seven ()`, and `raw seven` then `()` on the next line | both a call: exits 7 |
| `arr [2i64]`, and `arr` then `[2i64]` on the next line | both an index: exits 9 |
| `wild int8->` then `:mem = alloc(32i64);` on the next line | a binding: exits 0 |
| `mem` then `[3i64] = 5i8;` on the next line, and `mem [3i64]` | an index of it: exits 5 |
| `b.ptr [2i64]`, and `b.ptr` then `[2i64]` on the next line, over a `buffer` | an index: exits 9 |
| `error` then `:EOops;` on the next line; `error :EOops;`; `pub` then `error:EOops;`; `error:EOne; error:EOops;` | each a declaration: `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks for `EOops`' arm |
| `fixed` then `int64:X_MAX = 7i64;` on the next line | a bound: exits 7 |
| `fixed int64` then `[3]:T = […];` on the next line | a table: exits 5 |
| `struct` then `:Row = { int64:id; };` on the next line | a struct: exits 3 |
| `func` then `:main = …` on the next line | a `main`: exits 8 |

**And through the checks, planted** (the same `shapes.py`):

| Row | Shape | Check | HEAD's harness |
|---|---|---|---|
| T1 | `pass mono_now ();` | `check_purity` | **silent** — it matched the spelling `mono_now(` |
| T2 | `pass environ` then `();` on the next line | `check_purity` | **silent** |
| T3 | `pass v.items` then `[0i64];` on the next line, in `cal` | `check_raw_index` | **silent** — it matched `.items[` |
| T4 | `pass b.body.ptr [0i64];` in `cal` | `check_raw_index` | **silent** |
| T5 | `wild int64->` then `:p = v.items;`, and `p` then `[4i64]` | `check_raw_index` | **silent** — neither the binding nor its index is on one line |
| T6 | `error` then `:ETimeOops;` | `check_error_budget` | **silent** — it matched a line's start |
| T7 | `pub error :ETimeOops;` | `check_error_budget` | **silent** |
| T8 | `pub error:ETimeValue; pub error:ETimeOops;` | `check_error_budget` | **silent** — the second is not at a line's start |
| T9 | `fixed` then `int64:YEAR_MAX = 9999i64;` in `cal` | `check_constants_named` | **silent** |
| T10 | `struct` then `:Row = { int64:id; string:name; };` and a table of `Row` | `check_no_owning_fields` | **silent** — the struct is never read |
| T11 | `pub fixed string` then `[2]:NAMES = […];` | `check_no_owning_fields` | **silent** |

**Eleven of eleven silent at HEAD; each red with PD-51 applied.** The
self-check plants nine of them — T4 and T7 are T3's and T6's patterns with a
space where those have a line end. And `run._verdict`, which decides whether
an exempt file is a program, looked for the substring `func:main`: a `main`
spelled across a line end read as `none`, and the self-check's fourth verdict
specimen is that `main`.

### 1.5 The fifteen text-mode reads, and Python's line ends

0.1.4c counted fifteen `open(…, "r")` sites in `harness/`. **They are three
kinds**, measured at HEAD by `facts.py` part D and read one by one:

| Kind | Sites | What each reads |
|---|---|---|
| **Nitpick source, read as code or markers** | `build.py` `imports_of`; `arms.py` `code_only`; `run.py` `_verdict`; `checks.py` `code_lines`, `check_literal_divisors`, the umbrella's re-export count; `stages.py` `read` | a `.npk` file — **seven, each through `lexical.read` since PD-50** |
| **Every kind of file, scanned for a number or a citation** | `checks.py` `check_specs_current` and `check_denominators` | `.md`, `.npk`, `.py`, `.toml`, `.txt` — **both through `lexical.read` since PD-50**, so a line number here is `git grep -n`'s for every kind of file |
| **Not Nitpick source** | `build.py` (the emitted `.ll`); `manifest.py` (`nitpick.toml`); `checks.py` `budget_from_spec` (`SAFETY.md` §2) and `check_specs_current`'s declaration sources; `selfcheck.py`'s wrapper (its `.ll`) and `_run` (the verdicts file) | **six, left as text** — each file is written by a tool or by hand as text, and none is read for a line number the compiler gives |

**`newline=""` alone would not have been the fix.** The bytes `a` CR `b` LF `c`
LF are TWO lines to the compiler; read five ways (`facts.py` part D): text mode
gives `'a\nb\nc\n'`; `newline=""` keeps the CR, and then `.splitlines()` —
which two of the seven source sites call, `code_lines` and `stages.read` —
gives THREE lines; iterating a `newline=""` file — which `imports_of` and the
re-export count do — gives three; `newline="\n"` iterated gives two; and
bytes split at LF alone give two. `lexical.read` is the last: bytes, one
character per byte, and every caller splits at `"\n"`.

### 1.7 The `sweep` stage's headroom

0.1.4's §15 handed the close *"the sweep stage's headroom — 4.5 s at 0.1.2,
16.7 s at 0.1.3b, about 22 s here, against a 30 s threshold that the next
member, 0.2's `Timestamp` sweep, will reach, so the close says whether the
threshold or the stage changes."* **Measured at `c970483`**: the six members
take 2.8 + 2.0 + 8.1 + 0.5 + 5.5 + 3.6 = **22.5 s**, both legs, compile
included — 7.5 s under the threshold. **Neither changes at the close**: the
stage is under its threshold, and the threshold is the trigger it always was.
**What the close says instead, in `BUILD.md` B-9's dated note and cycle 0.2's
README**: the next member — 0.2.2's round trip over every second of 512 days,
about 44 million cases — is the one that crosses 30 s, so 0.2.2's plan measures
it before it lands and chooses among (a) the stage whole, and B-9 amended to
its measured cost; (b) the member's −O2 leg over a sample; (c) its domain split
across the two legs — **with (a) the default if the whole stage stays under
60 s**, because B-9b already makes CI run the stage whatever it costs, and the
exhaustive gate is this library's strongest claim.

## 2. The decisions, and what chose each

Three, drafted here and recorded by the worker in `../../DECISIONS.md` as the
next three TM numbers (§5.7 is their full text, alternatives included; §11
lists them).

- **PD-50 — the harness reads `.npk` source as the compiler's lexer does,
  through one module**: `harness/lexical.py`, `nitpick-regex`'s reader, its
  code ported statement for statement (its syntax tree equal with every
  docstring removed, `lexsame.py`), its docstrings this repository's. *Declined:*
  `newline=""` at the fifteen sites — measured insufficient (§1.5), and it
  leaves eight shapes; bytes at the seven source sites with the scanners kept —
  closes the CR and nothing else; a reader of this repository's own — a second
  answer to a question the sibling has answered and had audited twice; a
  subcycle of its own — §"Why".
- **PD-51 — every tree check matches TOKENS across the lexer's whitespace,
  over a file's whole blanked text.** *Declined:* `check_purity` alone — the
  most important check, but `check_raw_index` guards the one bound a bare
  pointer has, and one rule for every check is simpler than five exceptions;
  the limit stated and the fix deferred — the fix needs no rule invented, and
  each check's reading is rewritten for PD-50 in the same edit; Python's `\s`
  — it matches characters the lexer refuses.
- **PD-52 — the two checks cycle 0.0 deferred to this cycle are re-homed**:
  `check_check_registry` to the next subcycle that moves the check family,
  before it moves it; the `#wild_slice` length check to the first bare-pointer
  accessor outside `src/core/`, or the hardening cycle 0.8. *Declined:* both
  built at the close — each adds a live check and moves V-1a's arithmetic and
  the self-check's counts at the close whose totals the audit reads, and
  neither has a subject the close changes; both declined — the registry
  check's argument got stronger in this cycle.

| **2** | `cycle 0.1.5: the first half's record, READY-TO-CLOSE — CI run <id> at c970483` — the execution record, the findings and what the cycle taught, the REPORT (`READY-TO-CLOSE`), the cycle README's boxes, `ROADMAP.md`'s row | the first-half worker | | unchanged | yes |
| — | *the cycle audit, `npk:auditor`, filed as the workbench's `meta/audits/nitpick-time-0.1-<date>.md`* | the orchestrator | | | |
| **3** | `cycle 0.1.5: the audit's findings — …` — every finding fixed or declined, in the record's triage table | the second-half worker | triaged | `GREEN` | yes |
| **4** | `cycle 0.1.5: cycle 0.1 closed — the audit accepted, archived to done/0.1/, cycle 0.2 opens` — the archive, cycle 0.2's README and `0.2.0.md`, the close's texts, the record and its REPORT (`DONE`) | the second-half worker | archived | `GREEN` | yes |

- **Commit 2 exists because a CI result cannot live inside the commit that
  caused it**, and so does commit 4's own run — read after its push, and named
  in the REPORT as not in the record (`nitpick-regex`'s close did the same).
- **Commit 3 and commit 4 are two because a fix and an archive are two**: the
  triage's diff is read against the audit, finding by finding, and the
  archive's is fourteen renames and a ripple of path mentions — one commit
  would bury the fixes in the ripple. If the audit REFUSES the close (§8.3), commit 3
  is the dispatch's only commit and commit 4 waits for the next audit.
- **The push is the worker's**, as at 0.1.3c: `git -C "$REPO" push`, and
  nothing else — no force, no branch — unless the dispatch's `NOTES:` says the
  orchestrator pushes.
- **CI does not move its pin** in any of the four: `ci.yml` changes in its
  comments only, at commit 1, and the pin is the next re-pin's.

**If a commit's CI run is RED:** read the job's own log through
`gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/jobs/<job>/logs`
(`gh run view --log` can print nothing), never the summary (TM-146). A red the
local harness reproduces is a defect in the commit: fix it in a new commit and
push. A red it does not reproduce is an environment difference and a finding:
record it, fix forward, push. **Never re-run to get a green.**

---
## 4. The code, applied from this file

Group `code`, applied at step 2: `harness/lexical.py`, and the harness modules
that read through it. **No library code**: `src/` changes in §5, in two
comments of `src/core/core.npk`, line for line. §4.1 to §4.4 are in the order
`apply.py` applies them — 39 blocks.

### 4.1 `harness/lexical.py` — the reader (PD-50)

**A new file, 368 lines.** Its code is `nitpick-regex`'s `harness/lexical.py`
at `fb37391` — the blob `1148a2fd…` there, and the same blob at that
repository's `06d5c00`, `b0846db` and `0d26478`, so its later commits have not
moved it — **statement for statement**: `lexsame.py` (step 1 and step 2)
compares the two syntax trees with every docstring removed, and they must be
equal. **Its docstrings are this repository's**: the module's header says what
it mirrors, where it comes from and why it is here, citing this plan rather
than `nitpick-regex`'s decisions, and each function's docstring names the
callers in this harness. What it provides:

- `read(path)` — the file as bytes, one character per byte (latin-1), so every
  offset is the compiler's byte offset and nothing translates a CR;
- `spans(text)` — every comment and literal, as `(kind, start, end)`, by the
  lexer's rules: `//` to byte 10, `/* */` unnested, `"…"` ended by a newline,
  `""` empty and `"""` a block string, `r"…"` raw, `'x'` one code point, and a
  template's text but never its interpolations;
- `blank(text[, spans])` — those spans overwritten with spaces, every `\n`
  kept, so lines and columns are the file's own;
- `imports(text)` — every `use` that is code, as `(line, decoded path, pub)`,
  the path decoded as the compiler's `escape_decode` decodes it.

**What it does not mirror** is a section of its header: each shape it names —
a NUL byte, an interpolation nested more than eight deep, an invalid escape and
the like — is confined to files the compiler refuses outright, which the
`parse` stage turns red whatever the reader makes of them.

<!-- apply code: new harness/lexical.py -->
````python
"""THE HARNESS'S ONE READING OF NITPICK SOURCE -- cycle 0.1.5, PD-50.

EVERY `.npk` FILE THIS HARNESS OPENS IS OPENED BY `read()` HERE, and every
reading of one as CODE goes through `spans()`, `blank()` and `imports()`: the
import walk (`build.imports_of`, and through it `check_layering`, the library's
reach and the arm generator's subgraph), the arm generator's operator scan
(`arms.code_only`), the exemption verdict (`run._verdict`), the expectation
markers (`stages.read`), the two whole-tree scanners (`check_specs_current` and
`check_denominators`, whose line numbers are therefore `git grep -n`'s), the
umbrella's re-export count, and every tree check (`checks.strip_comments`,
`checks.blank_code`, `checks.code_lines`).

WHY, MEASURED AT CYCLE 0.1.5'S PLANNING, AT COMPILER `c970483`. Until then the
harness opened source in Python's TEXT MODE at seven sites, and blanked comments
and strings with two scanners of its own that knew `//` and `"` and nothing
else. The compiler's lexer does not read source that way, and eight shapes, each
planted in a scratch tree against the check it could fool, disagreed with it:
a lone CR ended a `//` comment here and not there, so what followed it was a
phantom import (`check_layering` RED over a program that imports nothing) or
opened a phantom string that blanked the next line's division
(`check_literal_divisors` silent); a `//` inside a `/* */` blanked the rest of
its line, hiding a clock call from `check_purity` and a fourth `error:` from
`check_error_budget`; a `'"'` character literal and a template's `//` each hid
a division; an escaped `use` path, `"..\\x2fhost/host.npk"`, hid an import of
`host` from `check_layering`, because the walk read the literal's TEXT and the
compiler reads its DECODED VALUE; and a `pub use` inside a `/* */` was
counted as a re-export (`check_denominators` RED over a count that was
right). Each form was run through the compiler too, as a program of its own,
with the same reading at `c970483` and `c3bdae2`, and the compiler's reading
is the one below. `0.1.5.md` section 1.3 has the table.

WHERE THIS MODULE COMES FROM. `nitpick-regex` met the same class at its cycle
0.0 close -- its fourth and fifth audits -- and answered it with this module
(its RX-157 and RX-165). THE EXECUTABLE CODE BELOW IS THAT REPOSITORY'S
`harness/lexical.py` AT ITS `fb37391`, STATEMENT FOR STATEMENT: `0.1.5.md`
compares the two files' syntax trees with every docstring removed, and they
are equal. The docstrings and the comments are this repository's; where they
cite a decision of `nitpick-regex`'s they say whose. One reader in two
libraries is one thing to re-read when the compiler's lexer moves.

WHAT IT MIRRORS, READ AT COMPILER `c970483` WITH `git show`, NOT FROM A
SUMMARY: `src/frontend/lexer.npk`'s `is_space`, `lexer_skip_trivia` and
`lexer_next`; `src/frontend/escapes.npk`'s `escape_decode` and
`decode_string`; and `p_parse_import` in `src/frontend/parse_decl.npk`.

  * THE TEXT IS BYTES. `read()` maps each byte to one character (latin-1), so
    every offset is the compiler's byte offset, `\\n` (byte 10) is the only
    line end, and nothing translates a CR. WHITESPACE is exactly the lexer's
    `is_space`: space, tab, CR and LF.
  * `//` runs to the end of the line -- to byte 10, whatever precedes it.
  * `/* ... */` does NOT NEST: the first `*/` closes it, and an unterminated
    one runs to the end of the file.
  * `"..."`: `\\` skips the byte after it; a NEWLINE ends the literal (the
    lexer's "newline in a string literal").
  * `""` followed by anything but `"` is an EMPTY string; three quotes open a
    BLOCK string, which closes at the first unescaped three -- the compiler's
    close since its 1.6.0 step 3e (DEF-98), in `c970483` and not in `c3bdae2`,
    which closed at the first `""`. No `.npk` here holds a block string.
  * `r"..."` is RAW -- no escapes, closed by the next `"` -- when the `r`
    begins a token.
  * `'x'`: a `\\` escape or ONE code point, then `'`; without the closing
    quote the lexer resyncs past the next `'` on the line, and so does this.
  * `` `...` ``: a TEMPLATE. Its text is literal and is not trivia -- a `//`
    in it is text -- and `&{` opens an interpolation that is CODE until the
    `}` that closes it at the brace depth it opened at.

WHAT IT IS NOT: a lexer. It finds the spans that are not code and nothing
else. `spans()` returns them; `blank()` replaces each with spaces, keeping every
newline, so a check's line and column numbers are the file's own; `imports()`
reads `use` declarations as `p_parse_import` does, and the path is the
literal's DECODED value.

WHAT IT STILL DOES NOT MIRROR, and each is confined to files the compiler
REFUSES -- which the `parse` stage, rooting every `.npk` in the tree at
`npkc`, turns red whatever this module reads in them: a NUL byte; an
interpolation nested more than eight deep; `_?`, `_!`, `_~`, `_^` at the start
of a template part; `e+r"` after a float; an invalid escape or UTF-8 sequence;
and a path that begins neither `./`, `../` nor `/`.

ITS OWN TEST IS THE SELF-CHECK'S PART E (`selfcheck.part_e`): one text holding
every form above, written to a FILE, read back through `read()`, and required
to give exactly the imports and the code the compiler would see -- and one
program holding every form a run can observe, compiled by the pinned `npkc`
and required to exit 0, so a re-pin that moves the lexer is a red run and not
a silent disagreement.
"""
import os
import re

_IDENT = frozenset("abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz"
                   "ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZ0123456789_")
# The lexer's `is_space`, exactly: space, tab, carriage return, newline. Python's
# `\s` also matches VT, FF, 0x1C-0x1F, 0x85 and 0xA0, which the lexer calls
# unexpected characters.
_WS = " \t\r\n"


def read(path):
    """A `.npk` file's text AS THE COMPILER READS IT (PD-50).

    BYTES, one character per byte (latin-1): every offset is the compiler's byte
    offset and no line end is translated, so `\\n` is the only one -- where
    Python's text mode (`newline=None`) had made a lone CR a line end and hidden
    code from the checks behind it. THE ONE WAY THE HARNESS OPENS A `.npk` FILE.
    Raises `OSError` like `open()`; the callers keep their own handling."""
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        return fh.read().decode("latin-1")


def _hex(c):
    """`hex_value` in `escapes.npk`: the digit's value, or -1."""
    if "0" <= c <= "9":
        return ord(c) - 48
    if "a" <= c <= "f":
        return ord(c) - 87
    if "A" <= c <= "F":
        return ord(c) - 55
    return -1


_SIMPLE_ESCAPES = {"n": 10, "r": 13, "t": 9, "\\": 92, '"': 34, "'": 39, "0": 0}


def _escape(s, at):
    """`escape_decode`: `(codepoint, bytes consumed)` for the escape at `s[at]`
    (a backslash), or None where the compiler's is invalid -- `LEX-BAD-ESCAPE`,
    and the file is refused."""
    if at + 1 >= len(s):
        return None
    e = s[at + 1]
    if e in _SIMPLE_ESCAPES:
        return _SIMPLE_ESCAPES[e], 2
    if e == "x":                               # \xHH -- exactly two
        if at + 3 >= len(s):
            return None
        h1, h2 = _hex(s[at + 2]), _hex(s[at + 3])
        if h1 < 0 or h2 < 0:
            return None
        return h1 * 16 + h2, 4
    if e == "u":                               # \u{...}
        if at + 2 >= len(s) or s[at + 2] != "{":
            return None
        i, acc, seen = at + 3, 0, False
        while i < len(s):
            if s[i] == "}":
                if not seen or acc > 0x10FFFF:
                    return None
                return acc, (i - at) + 1
            h = _hex(s[i])
            if h < 0:
                return None
            acc, seen, i = acc * 16 + h, True, i + 1
        return None                            # unterminated
    return None


def _decode(content):
    """`decode_string`: a plain literal's CONTENT (between its quotes, one
    character per source byte) to the BYTES of its value. An escape becomes its
    code point in UTF-8 -- `\\x2f` is `/`, and `\\xC3` is two bytes, U+00C3 --
    and an invalid one is skipped at its backslash, as the compiler does while
    refusing the file."""
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    while i < len(content):
        if content[i] == "\\":
            r = _escape(content, i)
            if r is None:
                i += 1
                continue
            out += chr(r[0]).encode("utf-8", "surrogatepass")
            i += r[1]
        else:
            out.append(ord(content[i]))
            i += 1
    return bytes(out)


# A `use` KEYWORD in blanked code: not the tail of an identifier, not a field
# (`x.use` -- a reserved word IS accepted as a field name, `nitpick-regex`'s
# RX-134), and not the head of a longer identifier.
_USE_KW = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.])use(?![A-Za-z0-9_])")
# `pub`, then any of the declaration modifiers `p_parse_decl` accepts between
# it and the keyword, then the end of the text before `use`. Whitespace is the
# lexer's (`_WS`), never Python's `\s`.
_PUB_BEFORE = re.compile(
    r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.])pub(?:[ \t\r\n]+(?:inline|noinline|comptime|async|thread))*[ \t\r\n]*$")


def spans(text):
    """Every comment and every literal, as `(kind, start, end)`, end exclusive.

    Kinds: `comment`, `string` (a plain `"..."`, which is the only kind a `use`
    may name), `raw`, `block`, `char`, `template` (a template's text and its
    backticks -- never its interpolations, which are code)."""
    out = []
    n = len(text)
    i = 0
    in_template = False
    interp = []            # the brace depth at each open `&{` -- the lexer's `interp_brace`
    brace = 0
    while i < n:
        if in_template:
            s = i
            while i < n and text[i] != "`" and not (
                    text[i] == "&" and i + 1 < n and text[i + 1] == "{"):
                i += 1
            if i >= n:
                out.append(("template", s, n))
                break
            if text[i] == "`":
                out.append(("template", s, i + 1))
                i += 1
                in_template = False
                continue
            if i > s:
                out.append(("template", s, i))
            i += 2                              # `&{` -- code from here
            in_template = False
            interp.append(brace)
            continue

        c = text[i]
        nx = text[i + 1] if i + 1 < n else ""
        if c == "/" and nx == "/":
            e = text.find("\n", i)
            e = n if e < 0 else e
            out.append(("comment", i, e))
            i = e
            continue
        if c == "/" and nx == "*":
            e = text.find("*/", i + 2)
            e = n if e < 0 else e + 2
            out.append(("comment", i, e))
            i = e
            continue
        if c == "}" and interp and brace == interp[-1]:
            interp.pop()                        # closes an interpolation: text again
            i += 1
            in_template = True
            continue
        if c == "{":
            brace += 1
            i += 1
            continue
        if c == "}":
            brace -= 1
            i += 1
            continue
        if c == "`":
            out.append(("template", i, i + 1))
            i += 1
            in_template = True
            continue
        if c == "r" and nx == '"' and (i == 0 or text[i - 1] not in _IDENT):
            e = text.find('"', i + 2)
            e = n if e < 0 else e + 1
            out.append(("raw", i, e))
            i = e
            continue
        if c == '"':
            if nx == '"':
                if i + 2 < n and text[i + 2] == '"':
                    # A BLOCK STRING CLOSES AT `"""` (DEF-98, the compiler's 1.6.0
                    # step 3e) -- through `c3bdae2` at the first `""`.
                    j = i + 3
                    while j < n:
                        if (text[j] == '"' and j + 2 < n and text[j + 1] == '"'
                                and text[j + 2] == '"'):
                            break
                        if text[j] == "\\":
                            j += 1
                        j += 1
                    e = min(n, j + 3)
                    out.append(("block", i, e))
                    i = e
                    continue
                out.append(("string", i, i + 2))      # `""`, the empty string
                i += 2
                continue
            j = i + 1
            while j < n:
                if text[j] == "\n":
                    break                             # the lexer stops the literal here
                if text[j] == '"':
                    j += 1
                    break
                if text[j] == "\\":
                    j += 1
                j += 1
            e = min(n, j)
            out.append(("string", i, e))
            i = e
            continue
        if c == "'":
            j = i + 1
            if j < n and text[j] == "\\":
                if j + 1 < n and text[j + 1] == "x":
                    j += 4
                elif j + 2 < n and text[j + 1] == "u" and text[j + 2] == "{":
                    k = text.find("}", j + 3)
                    j = n if k < 0 else k + 1
                else:
                    j += 2
            elif j < n:
                j += 1                                # ONE code point
            if j < n and text[j] == "'":
                e = j + 1
            else:
                e = j                                 # the lexer's resync
                while e < n and text[e] != "\n":
                    if text[e] == "'":
                        e += 1
                        break
                    e += 1
            e = min(n, e)
            out.append(("char", i, e))
            i = e
            continue
        i += 1
    return out


def blank(text, sp=None):
    """The text with every comment and literal replaced by spaces, NEWLINES KEPT,
    so offsets, lines and columns are the file's own. Interpolations stay: they
    are code, and a `/` in one arms `DivByZero` like any other."""
    chars = list(text)
    for _, s, e in (spans(text) if sp is None else sp):
        for k in range(s, e):
            if chars[k] != "\n":
                chars[k] = " "
    return "".join(chars)


def imports(text):
    """Every `use` declaration the compiler would read: `[(line, target, pub)]`.

    `text` is what `read()` returns. `target` is the string literal's DECODED
    value, as a filesystem path -- `os.fsdecode` of the bytes `_decode` gives,
    which is the path the compiler's `p_parse_import` takes. Until cycle 0.1.5
    this harness read the literal's TEXT (`build.imports_of`), so
    `"..\\x2fhost/host.npk"` was a directory named `..\\x2fhost` inside the
    importer's own layer, and an import of `host` passed `check_layering`. A `use`
    inside a comment, a string or a template is not code and is not an import."""
    sp = spans(text)
    code = blank(text, sp)
    trivia = {s: e for k, s, e in sp if k == "comment"}
    plain = {s: e for k, s, e in sp if k == "string"}
    out = []
    n = len(text)
    for m in _USE_KW.finditer(code):
        j = m.end()
        while j < n:
            if text[j] in _WS:
                j += 1
            elif j in trivia:
                j = trivia[j]
            else:
                break
        if j not in plain:
            continue                          # a logical path (`use std.x.*;`), or not an import
        target = os.fsdecode(_decode(text[j + 1:plain[j] - 1]))
        if not target:
            continue
        pub = _PUB_BEFORE.search(code, 0, m.start()) is not None
        out.append((text.count("\n", 0, m.start()) + 1, target, pub))
    return out
````

### 4.2 The readers — every `.npk` through `lexical.py` (PD-50)

Sixteen blocks, in five modules. **Each is a read or a scan replaced, and each
says in its own comment what it was and why it changed**:

- `build.py` — `import lexical`; and `imports_of` becomes
  `lexical.imports(lexical.read(path))`, the path the decoded value (rows P6
  and P7 of §1.3).
- `arms.py` — `import lexical`, and `code_only` becomes
  `blank_code(lexical.read(path))`.
- `run.py` — `import lexical`, and `_verdict` reads the file through
  `lexical.read` and blanks it with `checks.blank_code` before it looks for a
  `main` (so a `func:main` inside a comment or a literal is not one).
- `stages.py` — `import lexical`, and `read` splits `lexical.read(path)` at
  `"\n"` alone, where `splitlines()` split at a lone CR as well.
- `checks.py` — `import lexical`; `strip_comments` and a new `blank_code`
  become `lexical.blank` over the comment spans and over every span; the old
  `strip_strings` goes, since `blank_code` is the only caller's need;
  `check_literal_divisors`' docstring states what its old limits turned out
  to be; `code_lines` reads through `lexical.read`; the umbrella's re-export
  count becomes the number of `pub` imports `lexical.imports` finds (row P8);
  and `check_specs_current` and `check_denominators`, which read every kind of
  file for a line number, read `lexical.read(…).split("\n")`, so their line
  numbers are `git grep -n`'s.

**The six reads left as text** are §1.5's third row, each named there.

<!-- apply code: old harness/build.py -->
````python
# `use "<path>".<what>;` and `pub use "<path>".<what>;` -- BUILD.md B-16: every
# import is relative until dependency roots are populated (O-N1).
_USE = re.compile(r'^\s*(?:pub\s+)?use\s+"([^"]+)"')


def imports_of(path):
    """The relative paths a single source file imports, in file order."""
    out = []
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
        for line in fh:
            m = _USE.match(line)
            if m:
                out.append(m.group(1))
    return out
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/build.py -->
````python
def imports_of(path):
    """The relative paths a single source file imports, in file order.

    `use "<path>".<what>;` and `pub use "<path>".<what>;` -- `BUILD.md` B-16:
    every import is relative until dependency roots are populated (O-N1).

    READ THE WAY THE COMPILER READS IT, SINCE CYCLE 0.1.5 (PD-50): the file by
    `lexical.read`, as bytes, and each path as its string literal's DECODED
    value, from a `use` that is code rather than one inside a comment, a
    literal or a template. Until then this was one regular expression over
    each line of a text-mode read, and it disagreed with the compiler both
    ways: a lone CR ended a `//` comment, so a `use` after it was an import the
    compiler never makes, and `"..\\x2fhost/host.npk"` was a directory named
    `..\\x2fhost` where the compiler reads `../host/host.npk`
    (`0.1.5.md` section 1.3, rows P6 and P7).
    """
    return [target for _, target, _ in lexical.imports(lexical.read(path))]
````

````python
# `strip_strings` -- blank double-quoted string bodies -- lives in `checks.py`
# since cycle 0.1.1 and is imported above: `check_literal_divisors` reads
# operators too, and one definition of "what is a string" serves both.


def code_only(path):
    """A file's text with comments AND string bodies blanked."""
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
        return strip_strings(strip_comments(fh.read()))
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/arms.py -->
````python
# `blank_code` -- every comment and every literal blanked -- lives in
# `checks.py` and is imported above: `check_literal_divisors` reads operators
# too, and one definition of "what is code" serves both. (From cycle 0.1.1 it
# was `strip_strings(strip_comments(...))`, two scanners that knew `//` and
# `"` and nothing else; since cycle 0.1.5 both halves are `lexical.py`'s,
# PD-50.)


def code_only(path):
    """A file's text with every comment and every literal blanked, read the
    way the compiler reads it (`lexical.read`, PD-50)."""
    return blank_code(lexical.read(path))
````

````python
def strip_comments(text):
    """Blank out `//` comments, respecting double-quoted strings.

    A CHECK OVER SOURCE MUST NOT READ PROSE, and in this tree that is not a
    hypothetical: `src/host/host.npk`'s header contains the word `mono_now()`
    while explaining the purity rule, and `src/lib.npk`'s contains
    `host_now_utc` while showing the shape a re-export line takes. A naive
    `grep` for either would fail this repository on its own documentation --
    twice, today, on the first run.

    Line structure is preserved (a comment becomes spaces, never disappears) so
    a finding can still cite a line number that matches the file.
    """
    out, i, n = [], 0, len(text)
    in_str = False
    while i < n:
        c = text[i]
        if in_str:
            if c == "\\" and i + 1 < n:
                out.append(c)
                out.append(text[i + 1])
                i += 2
                continue
            if c == '"':
                in_str = False
            out.append(c)
            i += 1
            continue
        if c == '"':
            in_str = True
            out.append(c)
            i += 1
            continue
        if c == "/" and i + 1 < n and text[i + 1] == "/":
            j = text.find("\n", i)
            if j < 0:
                j = n
            out.append(" " * (j - i))
            i = j
            continue
        out.append(c)
        i += 1
    return "".join(out)


def strip_strings(text):
    """Blank double-quoted string bodies, preserving length and line structure.

    OPERATOR DETECTION CANNOT READ STRINGS. `use "./cal/cal.npk".*;` contains a
    `/` and a `*` and arms nothing; a scanner that counted them would charge
    every module in the library for a division it does not perform. Blanking the
    body rather than deleting it keeps every line number and column honest.

    (HERE SINCE CYCLE 0.1.1, moved from `arms.py`, which imports it: the S-6
    generator and `check_literal_divisors` both read operators, and two copies
    of "what is a string" are two answers to it. `strip_comments` above keeps
    string bodies, and `code_lines` below is comments-only, so every check that
    predates this move reads exactly what it read before.)
    """
    out, i, n, in_str = [], 0, len(text), False
    while i < n:
        c = text[i]
        if in_str:
            if c == "\\" and i + 1 < n:
                out.append("  ")
                i += 2
                continue
            if c == '"':
                in_str = False
                out.append(c)
            else:
                out.append(" " if c != "\n" else "\n")
            i += 1
            continue
        if c == '"':
            in_str = True
        out.append(c)
        i += 1
    return "".join(out)


def code_lines(path):
    """`[(lineno, code_only_text)]` for a source file, comments blanked.

    COMMENTS ONLY -- a string's body is kept, whatever `check_civil_literal`'s
    docstring said while it lived (cycle 0.1.0 to 0.1.0c: "blanks comments and
    string bodies"; it never did). A check that must not read strings either
    calls `strip_strings` itself, as `check_literal_divisors` does.
    """
    with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as fh:
        text = fh.read()
    return list(enumerate(strip_comments(text).splitlines(), 1))
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/checks.py -->
````python
def strip_comments(text):
    """Blank every comment, and nothing else: a literal's body is KEPT.
    `text` is what `lexical.read` returns.

    A CHECK OVER SOURCE MUST NOT READ PROSE, and in this tree that is not a
    hypothetical: `src/host/host.npk`'s header contains the word `mono_now()`
    while explaining the purity rule, and `src/lib.npk`'s contains
    `host_now_utc` while showing the shape a re-export line takes. A naive
    `grep` for either would fail this repository on its own documentation --
    twice, today, on the first run.

    Line structure is preserved (a comment becomes spaces, never disappears) so
    a finding can still cite a line number that matches the file.

    THE SPANS ARE `lexical.py`'s SINCE CYCLE 0.1.5 (PD-50): `//` to byte 10
    alone, `/* */` unnested, and no comment found inside a literal the
    compiler reads -- a character literal and a template's text included. Until
    then this was a scanner of its own that knew `//` and `"` and nothing else,
    so a `//` inside a `/* */` blanked the code after the block on its line,
    and a `'"'` opened a string that never closed (`0.1.5.md` section 1.3).
    """
    return lexical.blank(text, [s for s in lexical.spans(text)
                                if s[0] == "comment"])


def blank_code(text):
    """Blank every comment AND every literal -- a string, a raw or block
    string, a character, and a template's text but never its interpolations,
    which are code -- keeping every newline, so line and column numbers are the
    file's own. `text` is what `lexical.read` returns.

    OPERATOR DETECTION CANNOT READ LITERALS. `use "./cal/cal.npk".*;` contains
    a `/` and a `*` and arms nothing; a scanner that counted them would charge
    every module in the library for a division it does not perform. Blanking
    the body rather than deleting it keeps every line number and column honest.

    (Until cycle 0.1.5 this was `strip_strings(strip_comments(...))`, moved here
    from `arms.py` at cycle 0.1.1 so that the S-6 generator and
    `check_literal_divisors` shared one definition of "what is a string" -- a
    definition that knew `"` and nothing else. Since PD-50 it is
    `lexical.blank`, and the two still share it.)
    """
    return lexical.blank(text)


def code_lines(path):
    """`[(lineno, code_only_text)]` for a source file, comments blanked.

    COMMENTS ONLY -- a literal's body is kept, whatever `check_civil_literal`'s
    docstring said while it lived (cycle 0.1.0 to 0.1.0c: "blanks comments and
    string bodies"; it never did). A check that must not read literals calls
    `blank_code` itself, as `check_literal_divisors` does. The file is read by
    `lexical.read` and split at `\\n` alone since cycle 0.1.5 (PD-50), where a
    text-mode read and `splitlines()` had split at a lone CR as well.
    """
    return list(enumerate(strip_comments(lexical.read(path)).split("\n"), 1))
````

### 4.3 The tokens — whole-text patterns across the lexer's whitespace (PD-51)

Sixteen blocks, thirteen in `checks.py` and three in `run.py`. **`_W`**, the
lexer's whitespace as a character class, and **`_line(text, at)`**, a finding's
line from an offset, come first; then each check's pattern, rewritten to run
over the file's whole blanked text:

- `check_error_budget` — `_ERROR_DECL` finds `error` and its `:` wherever the
  declaration starts, however spaced, a second on one line included (T6, T7,
  T8), matched over `blank_code`, so a literal holding `error:` is not one;
- `check_constants_named` — `_BOUND_DECL`, `fixed` and its type and name
  across line ends (T9);
- `check_no_owning_fields` — `_FIXED_TABLE` and `_STRUCT_DECL` find a table and
  a struct declaration across line ends (T10, T11), and `_absorb` reads a
  struct's body from an offset, a field spanning lines joined into one text;
- `check_raw_index` — `_WILD_BINDING` finds a bare-pointer binding across line
  ends, and `_RAW_INDEX` an owned pointer's `[` after the lexer's whitespace
  (T3, T4, T5);
- `check_purity` — `_PURITY_CALL` matches a banned NAME, then whitespace, then
  `(`, over the file's comment-blanked text (T1, T2);
- `run._verdict` — `_MAIN` finds `func`, `:` and `main` however spaced, and
  `import re` joins `run.py`'s imports for it.

**Each finding still names a line**, the line its first token is on, so every
message the harness prints about the tree is unchanged — step 5 compares the
whole run.

<!-- apply code: to harness/checks.py -->
````python
# THE LEXER'S WHITESPACE, AS A PATTERN (PD-51): space, tab, CR and LF --
# `lexical._WS`, which is the compiler's `is_space`. Since cycle 0.1.5 every
# check's pattern runs over a file's WHOLE blanked text and allows this between
# any two tokens it names, because the compiler does: `mono_now ()`, a `[` on
# the line after `v.items`, and `error` then `:ETimeOops` on the next line are
# a call, an index and a declaration to it (`0.1.5.md` section 1.4), and a
# pattern matched against one LINE saw none of them.
_W = r"[ \t\r\n]"


def _line(text, at):
    """The 1-based line of offset `at` in `text`, `\\n` the only line end."""
    return text.count("\n", 0, at) + 1


def code_lines(path):
````

````python
def _absorb(out, cur, rel, lineno, text):
    """Read `text` as struct-body text; return the struct still open, or None.

    Fields are separated by `;` and the body ends at the first `}`, so this is
    the same function for both spellings and there is only one place that knows
    what a field looks like.
    """
    close = text.find("}")
    body, closed = (text[:close], True) if close >= 0 else (text, False)
    for field in body.split(";"):
        field = field.strip()
        if field and ":" in field:
            out[cur].append((rel, lineno, field))
    return None if closed else cur
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/checks.py -->
````python
def _absorb(out, cur, rel, code, start):
    """Read `code` from `start` -- just past a declaration's `{` -- as struct
    body text: fields separated by `;`, the body ending at the first `}`. One
    function for every spelling, so there is one place that knows what a field
    looks like. A field's text is its tokens joined by single spaces, and its
    line is the line its first token is on (PD-51: a field may span lines).
    """
    close = code.find("}", start)
    end = close if close >= 0 else len(code)
    pos = start
    for field in code[start:end].split(";"):
        text = " ".join(field.split())
        if text and ":" in text:
            lead = len(field) - len(field.lstrip())
            out[cur].append((rel, _line(code, pos + lead), text))
        pos += len(field) + 1
````

````python
                # The remainder of the declaration line, past its opening `{`.
                # For `struct:X = {` that is empty and the body follows; for
                # `struct:X = { a; b; };` it is the whole body.
                rest = line[m.end():]
                brace = rest.find("{")
                rest = rest[brace + 1:] if brace >= 0 else ""
                cur = _absorb(out, cur, rel, lineno, rest)
                continue
            if cur is None:
                continue
            cur = _absorb(out, cur, rel, lineno, line)
    return out
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/checks.py -->
````python
    out = {}
    for rel in files:
        # THE WHOLE TEXT, NOT A LINE AT A TIME, SINCE CYCLE 0.1.5 (PD-51): the
        # declaration's tokens may stand on separate lines -- `struct` and
        # `:Row = {` compile as a struct, measured -- and the body is read from
        # its `{` to its first `}` wherever the line breaks fall, which is
        # what the single-line spelling above needed as well.
        code = blank_code(lexical.read(os.path.join(tree, rel)))
        for m in _STRUCT_DECL.finditer(code):
            out.setdefault(m.group(1), [])
            brace = code.find("{", m.end())
            if brace >= 0:
                _absorb(out, m.group(1), rel, code, brace + 1)
    return out
````

### 4.4 The self-check — seventeen rows, a specimen and part E

Six blocks in `harness/selfcheck.py`:

1. **`_write` gains `newline=""`**, so a planted lone CR is written as the one
   byte it is on every platform — the reader it tests reads bytes.
2. **Seventeen `PLANTED` rows**, each a violation and its clean control: the
   eight reader rows of §1.3 (P1 … P8, in the check each could fool, P7's and
   P8's GOOD columns the point of the row, since the old reader fired on
   them), and nine token rows of §1.4 (T1, T2, T3, T5, T6, T8, T9, T10, T11 —
   T4 and T7 are T3's and T6's patterns with a space for a line end). 23
   become 40.
3. **The verdict mechanisms' comment** names a fourth specimen,
4. **and the specimen**: `main_split.npk`, `func` and `:main` on two lines,
   whose verdict must be `run:0`.
5. **Part E**, the reader against the compiler's lexer. **E1** is
   `nitpick-regex`'s case 18, text for text — `_LEX_TEXT`, 23 lines holding one
   of each lexical form, each hiding a `use` and a `/` beside real ones, and
   `_LEX_IMPORTS`, the ten imports the compiler reads there — written to a
   FILE and read back, because a lone CR is lost in the read, before any scan
   begins; `lexsame.py` holds both values equal to the sibling's. **E2** is one
   program of every form a run can observe, `_FORMS_TEXT`, compiled and run by
   `run._verdict` at the pinned compiler: it exits 0 only when every form
   reads as `lexical.py` reads it, and 10 … 16 name the form that did not
   (`_FORMS_EXIT`); the reader must see the same program's statements as the
   ones that ran. **So a re-pin that moves the lexer is a red run here**, not a
   reader quietly mirroring a compiler that is gone.
6. **Part E's call**, after the verdict mechanisms, and its `ok` line.

````python
def _write(path, text):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        fh.write(text)
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/selfcheck.py -->
````python
def _write(path, text):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    # `newline=""` (cycle 0.1.5): a planted lone CR is written as the one byte
    # it is, on every platform, because the reader it tests reads bytes.
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)
````

````python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run(rep, root, steps):
````
<!-- apply code: to harness/selfcheck.py -->
````python
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# PART E -- the reader, against the compiler's lexer (cycle 0.1.5, PD-50)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# `lexical.py` is read by every tree check, the import walk, the arm generator,
# the exemption verdict and the markers, so a regression in it weakens all of
# them at once and reddens none: this part is the red it would otherwise not
# have. Two halves, and they answer different questions.
#
# E1 ASKS THE READER, AGAINST WHAT THE COMPILER READS: `nitpick-regex`'s case
# 18, TEXT FOR TEXT -- its `_LEX_TEXT` and `_LEX_IMPORTS` below are that
# repository's at its `fb37391` -- one of each lexical form that repository
# found to matter, each hiding a `use` and a `/` beside the ones that are real
# code, written to a FILE and read back through `lexical.read`, because a lone
# CR is lost in the READ, before any scanning begins. Its expectations are the
# compiler's reading at `c970483`, measured there (`0.1.5.md` section 1.3).
#
# E2 ASKS THE COMPILER, AGAINST WHAT THE READER MIRRORS: every form a RUN can
# observe, in one program the pinned `npkc` compiles and runs -- exit 0 when
# each form reads as `lexical.py` reads it, and a code naming the form when one
# does not -- read by the reader as well, which must see exactly the statements
# that ran. So a re-pin that moves the lexer is a red run here rather than a
# reader quietly mirroring a compiler that is gone.
_LEX_TEXT = (
    'mod:lexcase;\n'                                        # 1
    'use "./real_a.npk".*;\n'                               # 2  an import
    'pub use "./real_b.npk".name;\n'                        # 3  a re-export
    '// use "./line_comment.npk".*; a / b\n'                # 4
    '/*\n'                                                  # 5
    'use "./block_comment.npk".*; a / b\n'                  # 6
    '*/\n'                                                  # 7
    'string:s = "use \\"./in_string.npk\\" a / b";\n'       # 8
    'string:r = r"use a / b"; use "./after_raw.npk".*;\n'   # 9  an import
    'string:k = """\n'                                      # 10
    'use "./block_string.npk".*; a / b\n'                   # 11
    '""";\n'                                                # 12
    "char8:q = '\"'; use \"./after_char.npk\".*;\n"         # 13 an import
    'string:t = `use "./template.npk" a / b &{ n / 2 }`;\n' # 14 one `/` is code
    'pub /* gap */ use /* gap */ "./gapped.npk".*;\n'       # 15 a re-export
    'int64:x = y.use;\n'                                    # 16 a field, not a keyword
    '// see\ruse "./after_cr.npk".*; a / b\n'               # 17 a lone CR is not a line end
    '// note\r/* a / b\n'                                   # 18 ...so this `/*` is comment text
    'use "./after_cr_comment.npk".*;\n'                     # 19 an import
    'use "..\\x2freal_c.npk".*;\n'                          # 20 an import, `../real_c.npk`
    'use ".\\u{2F}real_d.npk".*;\n'                         # 21 an import, `./real_d.npk`
    'use "./back\\\\slash.npk".*;\n'                         # 22 an import, one backslash
    'string:bs = """a""b use "./in_block.npk".*; """; use "./after_block.npk".*;\n'  # 23
)
_LEX_IMPORTS = [(2, "./real_a.npk", False), (3, "./real_b.npk", True),
                (9, "./after_raw.npk", False), (13, "./after_char.npk", False),
                (15, "./gapped.npk", True), (19, "./after_cr_comment.npk", False),
                (20, "../real_c.npk", False), (21, "./real_d.npk", False),
                (22, "./back\\slash.npk", False), (23, "./after_block.npk", False)]


# THE FORMS A RUN CAN OBSERVE. Each guarded assignment after a form runs
# exactly when the form reads as `lexical.py` reads it; line 6's does not run,
# because the lone CR before it is not a line end.
_FORMS_TEXT = (
    "mod:lexical_forms;\n"                                     # 1
    "use \".\\x2flexical_six.npk\".*;\n"                       # 2  decoded: ./lexical_six.npk
    "func:seven = int32() never fails { pass 7i32; };\n"       # 3
    "func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {\n"                  # 4
    "    int32:a = 0i32;\n"                                    # 5
    "    // a lone CR is not a line end\r    a = 10i32;\n"     # 6  all of it comment
    "    if (a != 0i32) { exit 10i32; }\n"                     # 7
    "    /* x // y */ a = 1i32;\n"                             # 8  code after the block
    "    if (a != 1i32) { exit 11i32; }\n"                     # 9
    "    /* p /* q */ a = 2i32;\n"                             # 10 the block does not nest
    "    if (a != 2i32) { exit 12i32; }\n"                     # 11
    "    char8:q = '\"'; a = 3i32;\n"                          # 12 one character
    "    if (a != 3i32) { exit 13i32; }\n"                     # 13
    "    string:t = `x//y`; a = 4i32;\n"                       # 14 a template's text
    "    if (a != 4i32) { exit 14i32; }\n"                     # 15
    "    int32:b = raw seven ();\n"                            # 16 a call, spaced
    "    int32:c = raw seven\n"                                # 17
    "    ();\n"                                                # 18 a call across a line end
    "    if (b != c) { exit 15i32; }\n"                        # 19
    "    if ((raw six()) != 6i32) { exit 16i32; }\n"           # 20 the decoded import
    "    exit 0i32;\n"                                         # 21
    "};\n") + FAILSAFE
_FORMS_SIX = ("mod:lexical_six;\n\n"
              "pub func:six = int32() never fails { pass 6i32; };\n")
_FORMS_EXIT = {
    10: "a lone CR ended a `//` comment",
    11: "a `//` inside a `/* */` ended the line",
    12: "a `/* */` nested",
    13: "the character literal `'\"'` hid the code after it",
    14: "a `//` in a template's text ended the line",
    15: "a call's `(` could not follow whitespace",
    16: "the escaped `use` path was not decoded",
}


def part_e(rep, root, man, base, npkc, npkrt):
    """The reader against the compiler's lexer: E1 and E2 above."""
    import lexical
    # `run` imports this module; by the time this is called it is initialised.
    import run as run_mod

    problems = []
    where = os.path.join(base, "lexical")
    if os.path.isdir(where):
        shutil.rmtree(where)
    os.makedirs(where)

    # E1 -- the reader, on one text of every form, through a FILE.
    path = os.path.join(where, "lexcase.npk")
    with open(path, "wb") as fh:
        fh.write(_LEX_TEXT.encode("latin-1"))
    text = lexical.read(path)
    if text != _LEX_TEXT:
        problems.append("E1: the file read back is not the bytes written -- a "
                        "line end or a byte was translated on the way in.")
    got = lexical.imports(text)
    if got != _LEX_IMPORTS:
        problems.append("E1: the imports read are %r, and the compiler reads "
                        "%r." % (got, _LEX_IMPORTS))
    code = lexical.blank(text)
    if len(code) != len(_LEX_TEXT) or code.count("\n") != _LEX_TEXT.count("\n"):
        problems.append("E1: blanking moved a byte or a line.")
    lines = code.split("\n")
    for ln in (4, 6, 8, 9, 11, 17, 18):
        if "/" in lines[ln - 1]:
            problems.append("E1: line %d: a `/` inside a comment or a literal "
                            "survived the blanking." % ln)
    if lines[13].count("/") != 1:
        problems.append("E1: line 14: the template's text was read as code, "
                        "or its interpolation was not.")
    if "after_char" in lines[12] or "use" not in lines[12]:
        problems.append("E1: line 13: the `'\"'` literal hid the rest of the "
                        "line, or was not blanked.")
    if "use" not in lines[18]:
        problems.append("E1: line 19: the `/*` inside line 18's comment was "
                        "read as a block comment and hid the code after it.")

    # E2 -- the compiler, on every form a run can observe, and the reader on
    # the same file.
    _write(os.path.join(where, "lexical_forms.npk"), _FORMS_TEXT)
    _write(os.path.join(where, "lexical_six.npk"), _FORMS_SIX)
    text = lexical.read(os.path.join(where, "lexical_forms.npk"))
    got = lexical.imports(text)
    if got != [(2, "./lexical_six.npk", False)]:
        problems.append("E2: the reader's imports are %r, and the program "
                        "imports ./lexical_six.npk at line 2." % (got,))
    lines = lexical.blank(text).split("\n")
    if "10i32" in lines[5]:
        problems.append("E2: line 6: the reader read code after a lone CR in "
                        "a `//` comment.")
    for ln, stmt in ((8, "a = 1i32"), (10, "a = 2i32"), (12, "a = 3i32"),
                     (14, "a = 4i32"), (16, "seven ("), (17, "seven"),
                     (18, "();")):
        if stmt not in lines[ln - 1]:
            problems.append("E2: line %d: the reader blanked `%s`, which the "
                            "compiler runs." % (ln, stmt))
    bld = build_mod.Build(where, man, npkc, npkrt, os.path.join(where, "build"))
    out_dir = os.path.join(where, "build", "forms")
    os.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)
    verdict = run_mod._verdict(bld, where, "lexical_forms.npk", out_dir)
    if verdict != "run:0":
        code_ = int(verdict[4:]) if verdict.startswith("run:") else None
        problems.append(
            "E2: the pinned compiler's verdict on the forms program is %s, not "
            "run:0 -- %s. THE COMPILER'S LEXER HAS MOVED FROM WHAT `lexical.py` "
            "MIRRORS: re-read `src/frontend/lexer.npk` at the new pin and bring "
            "the reader to it, in the adoption that moved the pin."
            % (verdict, _FORMS_EXIT.get(code_, "it did not build and run")))
    return problems


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run(rep, root, steps):
````

````python
                   "%d specimen(s) for `_verdict`; exemption, defect corpus "
                   "and header sweep each driven RED and silent on the "
                   "control" % len(VERDICT_SPECIMENS)))

    problems = part_e(rep, root, man, base, npkc, npkrt)
    if problems:
        ok = False
        rep.fail("self-check: the reader", "%d disagreement(s) with the "
                 "compiler's lexer" % len(problems))
        for p in problems:
            rep.note("")
            for line in p.splitlines():
                rep.note(line)
    else:
        rep.say("  ok    %-46s %s"
                % ("the reader (lexical.py)",
                   "E1: %d lines of every form read back, %d import(s) as the "
                   "compiler reads them; E2: the pinned compiler runs every "
                   "observable form to 0"
                   % (_LEX_TEXT.count("\n"), len(_LEX_IMPORTS))))
    return ok
````

## 5. The documents, and the decisions

Groups `docs` (§5.1 to §5.6, 67 blocks) and `decisions` (§5.7, four
blocks), applied at step 3, then `renumber.py` (§5.8). **Every edit below
either states what the close changed or corrects a sentence that was false in
the tree at this plan's commit** — each such correction keeps the old words in
a dated note where they are worth keeping, and each was found by §6's sweeps
or by reading the page whole (§1.9). The cycle README's 0.1.5 row names
eleven of them; the rest are the same class, found by looking.

### 5.1 The pages a reader meets first

`README.md` — the status block (no longer *"No code yet"*, nor *"the compiler
itself is at cycle 1.5"*); the range *"enforced by the type system when the
compiler's `limit<Rules>` lands"* — it landed at the compiler's 1.5.2, and this
library uses it on its containers' lengths, while the calendar's range stays
the constructors' to refuse; the arm bill, three identities and **11** and
**13** arms in all; *"What it will provide"*, which the calendar row now
provides; and the `tools/` line. `CLAUDE.md` — the status paragraph, the
harness paragraph (eleven modules, the reader and the tokens), and the
`tools/` line. `CONTRIBUTING.md` — *"Where it stands"*, and the accessor rule,
which is the slice guard since TM-129 and not *"in code"*. `nitpick.toml` —
the refusal split's *"every probe added since carries `expect-exit:`"*, false
since `probe14`, and the `check` entry's *"— cycle 0.0.4"*: the library's own
refusals are probes under `tests/probe/`, dispatched by their headers, and
cycle 0.2.0's refusal of a conversion between `Instant` and `Timestamp` is the
next.
