<!-- apply docs: old README.md -->
````markdown
**Overflow traps rather than wrapping.** Adding a century to a timestamp that
cannot hold one is a controlled stop in this language, not a silent journey to
the year 292 billion. The representable range is stated, checked at every
constructor, and enforced by the type system when the compiler's `limit<Rules>`
lands.
````
<!-- apply docs: to README.md -->
````markdown
**Overflow traps rather than wrapping.** Adding a century to a timestamp that
cannot hold one is a controlled stop in this language, not a silent journey to
the year 292 billion. The representable range is stated and checked at every
constructor, before the trap: a date outside it is refused, not built. *(This
said, until cycle 0.1.5, that the range would be "enforced by the type system
when the compiler's `limit<Rules>` lands". It landed at the compiler's 1.5.2,
and `ntime` uses it on its containers' lengths — TM-156 — while the calendar's
range stays the constructors' to refuse, `meta/specs/CALENDAR.md` C-8.)*
````

<!-- apply docs: old README.md -->
````markdown
**Importing it costs a consumer three `failsafe` arms — and one if all you want
is calendar arithmetic.** In this language every error identity a library
declares is a mandatory arm in every consuming program's shutdown handler, so
the number is an API decision, not an implementation detail. `ntime`'s is
three.
````
<!-- apply docs: to README.md -->
````markdown
**Importing it costs a consumer at most three error identities — and one if all
you want is calendar arithmetic.** In this language every error identity a
library declares is a mandatory arm in every consuming program's shutdown
handler, so the number is an API decision, not an implementation detail.
`ntime`'s is three. **The whole bill is larger**, because the language also
charges an arm for each kind of trap the imported code can reach — a division,
an overflow, an index, a loop's measure, a length's limit — on top of a floor
every program owes: measured at compiler `c970483`, a program importing only
the calendar owes **11** arms, and one importing the whole library **13**. The
table is `meta/specs/SAFETY.md` S-4, generated and checked against the
compiler on every run. *(Until cycle 0.1.5 this paragraph said importing
`ntime` "costs a consumer three `failsafe` arms", which counted the identities
and not the bill.)*
````

````markdown
**Status, after cycle 0.1.5: cycle 0.1's close — the harness reads source as

the close's planning, eight shapes of source made the harness disagree with
the compiler's lexer at `c970483`, and six of them passed a violation silently:
a lone CR in a `//` comment, a `//` inside a `/* */`, a `'"'` character
literal, a `//` in a template's text and an escaped `use` path each hid a
division, a clock call, a fourth `error:` or an import of `host` from the check
built to find it; the other two read a comment as code. **Since PD-50 every
`.npk` the harness opens is read by `harness/lexical.py`** — `nitpick-regex`'s
reader, ported statement for statement — as bytes, its comments and literals
by the compiler lexer's rules and its import paths decoded; **and since PD-51
every check matches TOKENS across the lexer's whitespace** over a file's whole
text, where `mono_now ()`, a `[` on the line after `v.items`, and `error` then
`:ETimeOops` on the next line had each hidden from a pattern matched against
one line. The self-check plants 40 tree-check violations (23 before) and, in
its part E, asks both the reader and the pinned compiler about one text of
every form, so a re-pin that moves the lexer is a red run. **And the close's
corrections**: the public `README.md` no longer says there is no code, nor that
importing `ntime` costs three arms — the calendar costs 11 and the umbrella 13
(`SAFETY.md` S-4); `tests/probe/README.md` lists every probe; and the two
checks cycle 0.0 deferred to this cycle, which it did not build, are re-homed
with their reasons (PD-52). No library code changed. A full invocation is
**112 units green** at pin `c970483`.
````

````python
    WHY HERE AND NOT AS A `[[test]]` ENTRY.  The manifest selects by DIRECTORY
    with a non-recursive glob (`<path>/*.npk`), and this corpus is a file and
    eight subdirectories (six when this was written, at cycle 0.0.6; seven
    since 0.1.3b's `fixed_move_out/`, eight since 0.1.4c's
    `fixed_import_scope/`); a recursive entry over `tests/probe/` would also
    sweep `support/`, which must never be run.  One entry per subdirectory
    would put the coverage rule in eight places that can drift apart.  This walks
````

### 5.4 The specifications

`SAFETY.md` — the *"Where `List<T>` is"* paragraph's note (the cycle README's
item from 0.1.3c, and TM-113's marker in §5.7); S-4b's *"The totals column is
generated at cycle 0.1"*, done; and S-4's amendment note, re-measured.
`meta/specs/README.md` — `VERIFICATION.md`'s row, which said the obligations
are carried *"into the compiler's cycle 1.5"*. `BUILD.md` — B-15's older note,
which still promised `check_public_prefix`, withdrawn at 0.1.3; and **B-9's
dated note on the `sweep` stage's headroom** (§1.7). `TESTING.md` — the
`check_purity` row's ban list, matched as calls; the `check_literal_divisors`
row's stated limits, which the measurement reversed; **the two new rules,
V-1k (PD-50) and V-1l (PD-51)**; the self-check table's part D row (four
specimens) and a part E row; V-14's calibration note (six, seven and ten since
`c3bdae2`); and **V-14e's note for PD-52**. `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — **O-X6's
note**: its *"0.3 by `ROADMAP.md`"* is not what the roadmap says, and three
places disagree about when `check_int128_sites` turns on (§8.5 hands the order
to cycle 0.2's opening); and O-X9's note, the two spellings `check_purity` did
not refuse, which make its recommendation's case.

````markdown
compiler generates for a `Vec` frees nothing, because its one owning field
holds no element and `items` is a bare pointer the walk does not follow

element-lifetime obligation is `ntime`'s.)*
````

````markdown
## 3. The exhaustive gates
````
<!-- apply docs: to meta/specs/TESTING.md -->
````markdown
the line's start, because the same words appear in header prose.

**Rule V-1k (PD-50) — the harness reads `.npk` source as the compiler's lexer
does, through ONE module.** `harness/lexical.py` is the only way the harness
opens a `.npk` file and the only reading of one as code: bytes, one character
each, `\n` the only line end and CR whitespace; `//` to the line's end; a
`/* */` unnested; a string closed by `"` and ended by a newline; raw, block and
character literals and a template's text by the lexer's rules; and a `use`
path as its literal's DECODED value. It is `nitpick-regex`'s reader, its code
ported statement for statement, so the two libraries' harnesses read source
one way. **Measured at cycle 0.1.5's planning, compiler `c970483`: before it,
eight shapes made this harness disagree with the compiler, and six passed a
violation silently** — a lone CR in a `//` comment, a `//` inside a `/* */`, a
`'"'`, a `//` in a template's text and an escaped `use` path each hid a
division, a clock call, a fourth `error:` or an import of `host` from the check
built to find it (`meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md` section 1.3). What it mirrors, and
the compiler commit it was read at, is its own header's; **the self-check's
part E asks both sides on every run** — the reader, one text of every form, and
the pinned compiler, one program of every form a run can observe — so a re-pin
that moves the lexer is a red run and not a stale mirror. The files the harness
reads that are NOT Nitpick source — the manifest, the specifications the checks
diff against, the emitted IR, the verdicts file — are read as text, each named
in `0.1.5.md` section 1.5.

**Rule V-1l (PD-51) — a check matches TOKENS, not lines.** Every pattern a tree
check matches runs over the file's WHOLE blanked text and allows the lexer's
whitespace — a newline included — between any two tokens it names, because the
compiler does: `mono_now ()` is a call, a `[` on the line after `v.items` is an
index, `error` then `:ETimeOops` on the next line is a declaration, and so is a
second `error:` after a `;` on one line — each measured at `c970483`, and each
passed by the pattern matched against one line that stood here until cycle
0.1.5. A finding still names the line its first token is on. The five checks
whose patterns this rule rewrote — `check_purity`, `check_raw_index`,
`check_error_budget`, `check_constants_named`, `check_no_owning_fields` — and
`run._verdict` are each planted with a spelling across a line end (V-14c).

## 3. The exhaustive gates
````

<!-- apply docs: to meta/specs/TESTING.md -->
````markdown

| `selfcheck.part_e` (cycle 0.1.5) | `lexical.py`, the reader every check reads through (V-1k): one text of every lexical form written to a file and read back — `nitpick-regex`'s case 18, text for text — and one program of every form a run can observe, compiled and run by the pinned compiler, which must exit 0 while the reader sees exactly the code that ran. And seventeen more `PLANTED` rows: V-1k's eight and V-1l's nine |
````

````markdown
subcycle that adds or retires a check, the next time the family moves: cycle
0.2's `check_int128_sites`, when it goes live, builds `check_check_registry`
first. Until then the four statements of the family are re-read row by row at
every close, and agree at this one.)*
````

### 5.7 The decisions — three, and three markers

**Three markers**, each on the line after the heading it qualifies, where
`check_refs` looks for one: **TM-113**'s names TM-193 as having replaced two
of its phrases — *"three fields"* and *"gets none of that"*, the cycle
README's item; **TM-144**'s and **TM-145**'s name PD-52 as having replaced
their deferrals to cycle 0.1. **Then the three decisions**, in a new section
at the end of `DECISIONS.md`, each with its alternatives declined and what it
does not claim. *(This paragraph avoids the verb the markers carry: a
decision number written near that word in any tracked markdown file is a
claim `check_refs` holds the decision's heading to, and before step 3 the
headings carry no marker.)*

````markdown
# Cycle 0.1.5 — the close of cycle 0.1, ratified @DATE@

### TM-<PD-50> — the harness reads `.npk` source as the compiler's lexer does, through one module, `harness/lexical.py`: `nitpick-regex`'s reader, its code ported statement for statement

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.5 (PD-50). Adds `harness/lexical.py`; amends
`harness/build.py`, `arms.py`, `run.py`, `stages.py`, `checks.py` and
`selfcheck.py`; `TESTING.md` V-1k.** Carried to the close by cycle 0.1.4c —
its §1.7: fifteen text-mode `open(…, "r")` sites, no tracked `.npk` holding a
carriage return, and a fix recommended as *"bytes, or `newline=""`, with a
self-check plant"* — **and measured at its full extent at the close's
planning, at compiler `c970483`:**

- **The fifteen sites are seven readers of Nitpick source, two scanners that
  read every kind of file for a line number, and six readers of things that are
  not source** — the manifest, the specification the error budget is read from,
  the declaration sources of the citation check, the emitted IR twice, and the
  verdicts file (`meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md` §1.5).
- **`newline=""` alone is not the fix**, measured on Python 3.12.3: it keeps a
  carriage return in the text, and then `str.splitlines()` — which two of the
  seven call — and iterating the file — which two more do — split at it
  anyway. Bytes, split at `\n` alone, is the fix.
- **And the carriage return was one shape of eight.** The compiler's lexer, read
  at `c970483` with `git show` (`src/frontend/lexer.npk`: `is_space`,
  `lexer_skip_trivia`, `lexer_next`), ends a `//` comment at byte 10 alone and
  treats byte 13 as whitespace, closes a `/* */` at its first `*/` without
  nesting, reads `'"'` as one character and a template's text as text, and
  resolves a `use` path by its literal's DECODED value. This harness's two
  hand-written scanners knew `//` and `"` and nothing else, and its import
  walk read the literal's text. **Eight shapes, each planted through the
  check it could fool, and each form read by the compiler, a program of its
  own, the same way at `c970483` and at `c3bdae2`: six passed a violation the
  compiler compiles** — a division hidden from `check_literal_divisors` three
  ways, a clock call from `check_purity`, a fourth `error:` from
  `check_error_budget`, an import of `host` from `check_layering` — **and two
  fired where the compiler reads a comment**: a `use` after a lone carriage
  return, read as an import of `host`, and a `pub use` inside a `/* */`,
  counted as a re-export (the plan's §1.3).

**The decision.** *One module is the only way the harness opens a `.npk` file
and the only reading of one as code: `harness/lexical.py`, whose executable
code is `nitpick-regex`'s `harness/lexical.py` at its `fb37391`, statement for
statement — the two syntax trees equal with every docstring removed — and whose
docstrings are this repository's. `build.imports_of`, `arms.code_only`,
`run._verdict`, `stages.read`, `checks.strip_comments`, `checks.blank_code`
(which replaces `strip_strings(strip_comments(…))`), `checks.code_lines`, the
umbrella's re-export count and the two whole-tree scanners all read through it.
The self-check gains eight `PLANTED` rows, each a shape where the old reading
disagreed with the compiler, and a part E that asks both sides: the reader, on
one text of every lexical form written to a file and read back —
`nitpick-regex`'s case 18, text for text — and the pinned compiler, on one
program of every form a run can observe, which must exit 0 while the reader
sees exactly the code that ran.*

**Why port and not write.** The same class met `nitpick-regex` at its cycle 0.0
close — its fourth and fifth audits, and its RX-157 and RX-165 — and the module
it built answers every shape measured here, was read against the compiler's
lexer at `c3bdae2` and again at `c970483`, and has been audited since. One
reader in two libraries is one thing to re-read when the lexer moves.

*Alternatives declined:*
- **`newline=""` at the fifteen sites**, the recommendation as worded —
  measured insufficient above, and it leaves the other seven shapes.
- **Bytes at the seven source sites, the scanners kept** — closes the carriage
  return and nothing else: the `/* // */`, `'"'`, template and escaped-path
  shapes live in the scanners and the walk, not in the read.
- **A reader of this repository's own** — a second answer to a question the
  sibling has answered and had audited, and a second thing to re-read at every
  re-pin.
- **A subcycle of its own before the close** — the close carries the item by
  its README row; a close is where cycle 0.0.6 made its eight instrument
  repairs; and the audit that follows reads the reader as it reads everything
  else. **What it costs:** `lexical.py`'s 368 lines, sixteen edits in five
  modules, eight rows and a part in the self-check, and about a second on a
  full run of two minutes, TM-<PD-51> included — measured at planning.

**What it does not claim.** The reader mirrors the lexer's trivia and literals
and the parser's `use`, and no more; its header lists what it does not mirror,
each confined to files the compiler refuses. And it is not the checks: a check
that matched a spelling rather than a token stayed narrower than its name,
which is TM-<PD-51>.

### TM-<PD-51> — every tree check matches TOKENS across the lexer's whitespace, over a file's whole blanked text; never a line

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.5 (PD-51). Amends `harness/checks.py` — `check_purity`,
`check_raw_index`, `check_error_budget`, `check_constants_named`,
`check_no_owning_fields` — and `run._verdict`; `TESTING.md` V-1l.** Found at
planning, by the question TM-<PD-50>'s measurement raised: once the reader is
the compiler's, what does each check's PATTERN assume? Measured at `c970483`,
each shape with a program the compiler builds, runs or refuses by what it
reads (the plan's §1.4):

| Shape | The compiler reads | The check, matched per line |
|---|---|---|
| `mono_now ()`; a `(` on the line after `environ` | a call | `check_purity`: silent — it matched `name(` |
| `v.items` then `[0i64]` on the next line; `b.body.ptr [0i64]` | an index | `check_raw_index`: silent — it matched `.items[` |
| `wild int64->` then `:p = …` on the next line, and `p` then `[4i64]` | a binding and its index | `check_raw_index`: silent |
| `error` then `:ETimeOops;` on the next line; `error :ETimeOops;`; a second `error:` after a `;` | a declaration | `check_error_budget`: silent — it matched a line's start |
| `fixed` then `int64:YEAR_MAX` on the next line | a bound | `check_constants_named`: silent |
| `struct` then `:Row = {…}`; `pub fixed string` then `[2]:NAMES` | a struct; a table | `check_no_owning_fields`: silent |
| `func` then `:main = …` | a `main` | `run._verdict`: `none` |

**The decision.** *Every pattern a check matches runs over the file's WHOLE
blanked text — TM-<PD-50>'s reader — and allows the lexer's whitespace,
`[ \t\r\n]`, between any two tokens it names; a declaration is found wherever
it starts, not only at a line's start. A finding names the line its first
token is on. Nine `PLANTED` rows — each a spelling across a line end or a
space, or a second declaration on one line — and a fourth specimen for
`run._verdict`.*

*Alternatives declined:*
- **`check_purity` alone** — the most important check (V-1), and the one
  nothing else backs; but `check_raw_index` guards the one bound a bare pointer
  has (S-17b), and each of the other three is one pattern. One rule for every
  check is simpler to state and to audit than five exceptions.
- **The limit stated in each docstring, the fix deferred** — cycle 0.0.6
  deferred gaps whose fix needed a rule invented; this one needs none, since
  the lexer's whitespace is the rule, and each check's reading is rewritten for
  TM-<PD-50> in the same edit.
- **Python's `\s` between tokens** — it also matches VT, FF and 0x1C to 0x1F,
  which the lexer refuses as unexpected characters; the class is the lexer's
  `is_space`, as `lexical._WS` is.

**What it does not claim.** A check still matches only the tokens it names: a
banned call reached through an alias, and a bare pointer indexed under another
name in another function, stay `check_purity`'s and `check_raw_index`'s stated
limits (their docstrings; `../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` O-X9 for the first).

### TM-<PD-52> — the two checks cycle 0.0 deferred to cycle 0.1, which cycle 0.1 did not build, are re-homed with their reasons

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.5 (PD-52). Amends `TESTING.md` V-14e; replaces TM-144's and
TM-145's deferrals in part.** Found at planning by the close's promise sweep —
every live statement that cycle 0.1 would do something (the plan's §1.8): two
checks cycle 0.0's close deferred to this cycle, each with a reason, and
neither built.

- **`check_check_registry`** (TM-145, V-14e): `TESTING.md` §2's table,
  `checks.LIVE`, `checks.PENDING` and the checks `run.py` drives outside step 5
  are four statements of one family and nothing diffs them. Deferred because
  §2's table needed a machine-readable shape. **At this close the four agree**,
  re-read row by row, **and V-1a's arithmetic — the belt — went stale inside
  this cycle**, 17 and 13 against 18 and 14 from cycle 0.1.0 to 0.1.0b: the
  argument for the check got stronger, not weaker.
- **The `#wild_slice` length check** (TM-144): nothing checks a slice's length
  argument, and a read laid over `cap` would accept a dead slot. Deferred to
  *"Cycle 0.1, with `src/cal/` beside it"* — a second subject — **and `src/cal/`
  lays no slice**, so the second subject never arrived. Measured at the close's
  planning, the length is guarded by behaviour where a caller chooses the
  index: over `cap`, `vec_at_past_end` and `vec_set_past_end` each exit 11
  where they assert 94, and `bytes_view` over its capacity reddens
  `bytes_put_int`, `bytes_growth` and `bytes_view_lifetime`; `vec_pop`'s index
  is `count − 1` and cannot reach a dead slot.

**The decision.** *`check_check_registry` is built by the next subcycle that
adds or retires a check — the next time the family moves, which is when a drift
can happen — before it moves it; until then the four statements are re-read at
every close. The `#wild_slice` length check waits for its second subject, the
first accessor over a bare pointer outside `src/core/vec.npk` and `bytes.npk`,
and is the hardening cycle's, 0.8, if none arrives before it; until then the
units above are its guard.*

*Alternatives declined:* **both built at the close** — each adds a live check
and moves V-1a's arithmetic and the self-check's counts at the close whose
totals the audit reads, the registry check reads §2's first column as data —
a change to that document's form — and neither has a subject the close
changes; **both declined** — the registry check's argument got stronger in
this cycle, and the length check's gap is real for the next accessor.
````

### 5.8 The numbers — `renumber.py`

**PD-50, PD-51 and PD-52 become the next three TM numbers** — `199`, `200` and
`201` if the gate read `### TM-198` — and `@DATE@` the work commit's date.
0.1.3c's script, pointed at this plan's three labels.

<!-- script: renumber.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 (`0.1.5.md` §5.5, run at §7 step 3): the draft labels PD-50, PD-51 and
PD-52 become the next three TM numbers, in order, and `@DATE@` becomes the work
commit's date. In `meta/DECISIONS.md` the new section's headings, the two
supersession markers and the cross-references are written `TM-<PD-50>` ...;
each becomes its number, and the `(PD-50)` provenance labels stay, as TM-184's
`(PD-35)` does. EVERYWHERE ELSE outside this plan, every `PD-50` ... `PD-52`
becomes its number. `@DATE@` is replaced in `DECISIONS.md` and in every file
that carries one of the three labels AND IN NO OTHER: earlier plans quote
`@DATE@` in their hunks, and 0.1.4c's first draft of this script, which
replaced it everywhere, rewrote eleven dates in a record at rehearsal.
0.1.3c's `renumber.py`, pointed at this plan's three labels, reading and
writing with `newline=""` so no byte it was not asked to change moves.

    renumber.py FIRST_TM DATE

FIRST_TM is read by the plan's gate; DATE is the day of the work commit,
YYYY-MM-DD. Tracked AND untracked files -- `git grep` skips untracked files
unless told. Prints every file it changed and the counts; exit 1 if a label, a
placeholder or a date is left, or a heading is missing."""
import os, re, subprocess, sys

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
first, date = int(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2]
if not re.fullmatch(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}", date):
    sys.exit("STOP: DATE must be YYYY-MM-DD, not %r" % date)
pd0, count = 50, 3
MAP = {"PD-%d" % (pd0 + i): "TM-%d" % (first + i) for i in range(count)}
LABEL = re.compile(r"\b(?:%s)\b" % "|".join(re.escape(k) for k in MAP))
PATTERN = "|".join(r"PD-%d\b" % (pd0 + i) for i in range(count))
PLAN, DEC = "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md", "meta/DECISIONS.md"


def read(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def write(path, text):
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)


def grep():
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-l", "--untracked", "-E", PATTERN],
                         stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout.split()
    return [r for r in out if r != PLAN]


path = os.path.join(REPO, DEC)
dec = read(path)
placeholders = 0
for k, v in MAP.items():
    placeholders += dec.count("TM-<%s>" % k)
    dec = dec.replace("TM-<%s>" % k, v)
dates = dec.count("@DATE@")
dec = dec.replace("@DATE@", date)
write(path, dec)
print("  %-50s %d placeholder(s), %d date(s)" % (DEC, placeholders, dates))

changed, files = 0, [r for r in grep() if r != DEC]
for rel in files:
    p = os.path.join(REPO, rel)
    text = read(p)
    d = text.count("@DATE@")
    new, n = LABEL.subn(lambda m: MAP[m.group(0)], text.replace("@DATE@", date))
    write(p, new)
    changed += n
    print("  %-50s %d label(s), %d date(s)" % (rel, n, d))
left = [r for r in grep() if r != DEC] + [r for r in files if "@DATE@" in read(os.path.join(REPO, r))]
missing = [v for v in MAP.values() if not re.search(r"^### %s — " % v, dec, re.M)]
stale = "TM-<PD-" in dec or "@DATE@" in dec
print("labels renumbered: %d; PD-%d ... PD-%d -> TM numbers %d ... %d"
      % (changed, pd0, pd0 + count - 1, first, first + count - 1))
if missing:
    print("  STOP: meta/DECISIONS.md declares none of: %s" % ", ".join(missing))
if left:
    print("  STOP: labels or dates left in: %s" % ", ".join(left))
if stale:
    print("  STOP: a placeholder is left in meta/DECISIONS.md")
sys.exit(1 if missing or left or stale else 0)
````

---

## 6. The sweeps

Two, both by script, both run before anything changes (step 1) and after
everything is applied (step 4), so the counts the record carries are the
command's (the workbench PLAYBOOK §12). **And both run each question twice —
by identifier and by shape** — because 0.1.3c's reason-sweep found the one
live rule its id-sweep missed by a sentence that named nothing — the pass by
shape *"which the close's own sweep should reuse, running BOTH passes, by id
and by shape"*, the dispatch to this plan said.

### 6.1 The stale-claim sweep — every restatement, by id and by shape

For each thing this close restates — fifteen, S1 to S15 — `stale.py` greps
for statements of the OLD state over tracked and new files, the records
excluded, in two passes: **I**, the thing's identifiers (a number, a phrase, a
file name), and **P**, the shapes a sentence stating the old state takes
without naming it. **Every hit is read**, and after step 3 each must be one of
three kinds: **edited** — changed by this close and now dated or true;
**history** — a dated note, a struck entry's record, a ticked checklist item
or a cost quoted of an earlier pin; or **still true** — about something else.
**Any other is a stop.** The before and after counts are §7's blocks; the
table below is the reading of the after block, made at planning, one row per
file and pattern.

<!-- script: stale.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1 and step 4: THE STALE-CLAIM SWEEP (`0.1.5.md` §6.1), both
passes. For each thing the close restates, `git grep` for its OLD state over
tracked AND new files, the RECORDS excluded -- `meta/roadmap/done/`, every
subcycle plan of cycle 0.1 (this one's included), `meta/DECISIONS.md`'s settled
text, the defect and probe transcripts, and the tzdb spike's scratch -- by two
passes: I, the thing's IDENTIFIERS (a number, a count in words, a file name, a
phrase that names it), and P, the SHAPES a sentence stating the old state takes
without naming it, which is how cycle 0.1.3c's reason-sweep found the one live
rule its id-sweep missed. Every hit prints with its file and line; §6.1's table
says what each one is: EDITED by this subcycle, HISTORY, or STILL TRUE. A
decision this subcycle adds -- a number above TM-198 -- prints as `TM-(new)`,
because the plan quotes this output and `check_specs_current` reads a TM number
in a fence as a citation."""
import os, re, subprocess

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
EXCLUDE = [":!meta/roadmap/done/*", ":!meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.*.md",
           ":!meta/DECISIONS.md", ":!*TRANSCRIPT.txt", ":!meta/scratch/*",
           ":!tests/probe/*.txt"]
PATTERNS = [
    ("S1", "no code yet, planning status",
     r"No code yet|Status: planning",
     r"no code (exists|yet)|nothing computes anything|is planned now so that"),
    ("S2", "the arm bill as three, or one for the calendar",
     r"three .failsafe. arms|one if all you want",
     r"costs? a consumer (three|one) |owes (exactly )?one arm\b"),
    ("S3", "the floor as four arms",
     r"names four arms|floor of four|the four unconditional floor|floor is exactly",
     r"(the|its) floor (is|was) four|compiles and runs with four\s*$"),
    ("S4", "the reader's old shape",
     r"strip_strings|splitlines\(\)|text mode|newline=\"\"",
     r"knew `//` and|not modell?ed|blank(s|ed)? double-quoted|read(s)? .{0,20}line by line"),
    ("S5", "the `check` entry's cycle",
     r"— cycle 0\.0\.4\.$|-- cycle 0\.0\.4",
     r"gains an entry when the library"),
    ("S6", "the placeholders' count",
     r"other five placeholders|five sibling placeholders|five remaining",
     r"placeholders? point"),
    ("S7", "the defect corpus's subdirectories",
     r"six subdirectories|six places",
     r"subdirectories deep"),
    ("S8", "the tools/ and fixtures lines",
     r"the tzdb tables; everything they emit|tzdb's own test material",
     r"committed corpora"),
    ("S9", "`Vec`'s shape as three fields",
     r"three fields|ours verbatim|shape, verbatim",
     r"gets none of that|ordinary struct"),
    ("S10", "a check deferred to cycle 0.1",
     r"is cycle 0\.1's|Cycle 0\.1\*\*, with|check_check_registry",
     r"check_public_prefix|length argument"),
    ("S11", "the calibration's numbers and the plant count",
     r"\*\*4\*\*, the floor|costs \*\*8\*\*|[Tt]wenty-three planted|23 tree-check|23 planted",
     r"planted violations? across"),
    ("S12", "the compiler's cycle as current",
     r"cycle 1\.5 \(verification\)|moving through its cycle 1\.5|compiler.s cycle 1\.5 *\|",
     r"at cycle 1\.5\b"),
    ("S13", "O-N4's cost as current",
     r"281 s(econds)?|30\.9 GiB",
     r"580 MiB|is not runnable in CI"),
    ("S14", "O-X6's cycle",
     r"0\.3 by .ROADMAP",
     r"designs .period_add."),
    ("S15", "a cost per program",
     r"about 2\.5 s per program",
     r"s per program"),
]
total = 0
for tag, what, ident, shape in PATTERNS:
    for pas, rx in (("I", ident), ("P", shape)):
        out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-I", "--untracked", "-E", rx,
                              "--", "."] + EXCLUDE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                             text=True).stdout.splitlines()
        files = sorted({l.split(":", 1)[0] for l in out})
        total += len(out)
        print("%s%s %s: %d line(s) in %d file(s)" % (tag, pas, what, len(out), len(files)))
        for l in sorted(out, key=lambda x: (x.split(":")[0], int(x.split(":")[1]))):
            f, n, text = l.split(":", 2)
            text = re.sub(r"\bTM-(\d+)", lambda m: "TM-(new)" if int(m.group(1)) > 198 else m.group(0), text)
            print("    %s:%s: %s" % (f, n, re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text.strip())[:100]))
print("stale-claim sweep: %d line(s) over %d pattern(s), two passes each" % (total, len(PATTERNS)))
````

### 6.2 The reason-sweep — what the struck defects were cited FOR

**Every compiler defect this repository raised or carried that the registry
has struck** — cycle 0.1.3c's six at `c970483` (its passes I, D and P,
unchanged) and **the eight struck before them**, which 0.1.3c's sweep did not
cover: O-N4, O-N8, O-N9, O-N10, O-N11, O-N17, O-N18 and O-N19 (passes I2, by
id, and P2, by the shapes their sentences take). Striking a defect does not
retire what was written because of it. Each hit is RECORD — a plan or
execution record, a transcript, `DECISIONS.md`'s settled text — or LIVE, and
**every LIVE site is read for what it cites the defect FOR**: a history, a
regression test's reason, or a rule. **A live rule, check or test that still
gives a struck defect as a CURRENT reason is a stop** — none is left after
step 3; the table says what each LIVE file cites.

<!-- script: reasons.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1 and step 4: THE REASON-SWEEP (`0.1.5.md` §6.2) over every
compiler defect this repository raised or carried and the registry has struck
as discharged -- cycle 0.1.3c's six at `c970483` (its passes I, D and P,
unchanged) and the eight struck before them (passes I2 and P2, new at this
close). Striking a defect does not retire what was written BECAUSE of it; a
rule, a check or a comment that still gives the defect as its reason stands on
something gone. `git grep` over tracked AND new files, this plan excluded. Each
hit is RECORD -- a plan or execution record, a transcript, `DECISIONS.md`'s
settled text -- or LIVE, and §6.2's table says what each LIVE site cites the
defect FOR. The registry ids O-N21, O-N22, O-N24 and O-N26 are not defined in
this repository, so this script and the plan's quotation of its output are the
only places they are written (the workbench PLAYBOOK §6)."""
import os, re, subprocess

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
PLAN = "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.5.md"
RECORD = re.compile(r"^meta/roadmap/(done/|0\.1/0\.1\.[0-9][a-c]?\.md$)|TRANSCRIPT\.txt$|"
                    r"^meta/DECISIONS\.md$|^meta/scratch/|^tests/probe/probe04b_emission_shape\.txt$")
PASSES = [
    ("I", "the six struck at c970483 -- the registry's ids",
     r"\bO-N2[0-4]\b|\bO-N26\b", ("O-N20", "O-N21", "O-N22", "O-N23", "O-N24", "O-N26")),
    ("D", "the six -- the compiler's numbers", r"\bDEF-(99|102|104|105|106|108)\b", ()),
    ("P", "the six -- two shapes, stated as current",
     r"does not stop the move|not stop a (move|MOVE)|IMPORTING module's scope|"
     r"importing module's scope|compiles,? and (then )?faults", ()),
    ("I2", "the eight struck before them -- the ids",
     r"\bO-N(4|8|9|10|11|17|18|19)\b",
     ("O-N4", "O-N8", "O-N9", "O-N10", "O-N11", "O-N17", "O-N18", "O-N19")),
    ("P2", "the eight -- their shapes, stated as current",
     r"281 s(econds)?\b|30\.9 GiB|quadratic in the size|merges a sibling|"
     r"view escapes its|compares TAGS ONLY|derive\(Eq\)` on a payload enum will not|"
     r"ON A PAYLOAD ENUM DOES NOT COMPILE|O-N10 shows|O-N17 (is latent|blocks)|"
     r"`main` and no `failsafe` compiles|npk\.vacant|cannot be lowered|"
     r"not enforced inside a generic", ()),
]


def grep(rx):
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-I", "--untracked", "-E", rx, "--", ".",
                          ":!" + PLAN], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout.splitlines()
    return sorted(out, key=lambda l: (l.split(":", 2)[0], int(l.split(":", 2)[1])))


for tag, what, rx, ids in PASSES:
    hits = grep(rx)
    files = sorted({h.split(":", 1)[0] for h in hits})
    rec = [h for h in hits if RECORD.search(h.split(":", 1)[0])]
    print("%s %s: %d line(s) in %d file(s) -- %d RECORD, %d LIVE"
          % (tag, what, len(hits), len(files), len(rec), len(hits) - len(rec)))
    for ident in ids:
        h = grep(r"\b%s\b" % ident)
        print("    %-6s %3d line(s) in %2d file(s)" % (ident, len(h), len({x.split(":", 1)[0] for x in h})))
    per = {}
    for h in hits:
        per.setdefault(h.split(":", 1)[0], []).append(h.split(":", 2)[1])
    live = [f for f in sorted(per) if not RECORD.search(f)]
    if tag in ("P", "P2"):
        for f in live:
            print("    LIVE   %-58s %s" % (f, ",".join(per[f])))
    else:
        print("    LIVE in %d file(s): %s" % (len(live), " ".join(
            "%s:%d" % (f, len(per[f])) for f in live)))
print("reason-sweep: done")
````

**The after block, read at planning.** Passes I, D and P — the six — find
the same files and the same counts before and after step 3 (61, 52 and 12
LIVE lines; only line numbers move): **the close adds no mention of them**,
and 0.1.3c's §6.2 read every one. **The new passes' LIVE sites, every one read
at planning** (I2, 176 lines in 54 files — the 169 before and seven that this
close's own notes add — and P2, 49 lines in 21 files):

| What the LIVE sites cite a struck defect FOR | Where |
|---|---|
| **its struck entry and record** | `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`, each heading struck through with its decision; the defect README's rows, under its note *"Read this table as cycle 0.0.0's"* |
| **a regression test's reason** — the case that proves the fix, its old verdict kept as the control | every file of `defect/derive_payload_enum/`, `fixed_array_len/`, `generic_element_move/`, `generic_owning_copy/`, `missing_failsafe/` and `view_escape/`, and their READMEs, each titled or headed FIXED |
| **a lesson that outlived the defect** — O-N11's *"`npkc` exit 0 is not well-formedness"* | `ci.yml`, `build.py`, `manifest.py`, `selfcheck.py`, `nitpick.toml`, `BUILD.md` B-4b, `SAFETY.md` S-6b, `tests/conformance/README.md` |
| **a dated history** in a live file | `CLAUDE.md`; `run.py`'s exemption and corpus comments; `stages.py`; `SAFETY.md`'s O-N9, O-N17 and O-N19 notes; `TESTING.md`; `cal.npk`, `bytes.npk`, `limits.npk`, `vec.npk`, `lib.npk`; probes 04, 04b, 05b, 09, 10, 12, 12b and `civil_order` |
| **present tense, and this close dates it** | the defect README's O-N4 section and the probe README's *"it is O-N4's … case"* (O-N4); `probe05`'s item 3 and its `Clone` comment (O-N10); `generic_element_move/case2`'s *"is latent"* and `case3`'s *"blocks"* (O-N17) — §5.6 |

**None states a struck defect as a current reason after step 3.** Five did
before it, all in the last row — and none was a rule or a check: comments and
a README section, the class 0.1.3c's P pass found once and its I pass could
not.


---
## 7. The first half's steps, in order

Each step's expected output is the rehearsal's; the worker's own run is the
record, and **a difference is a finding, not a number to copy. Anything red
that a step does not predict is a stop.** Every block is compared by
`same.py`, a wall clock (`N.N s`) set aside. **`free -g` before each full run**
— the harness at step 0 and step 5, `facts.py` at step 1, `mut.py` at step 5 —
and nothing of this subcycle's in parallel with another.

**Step 0 — the start, and the baseline.** Set this file's title to
`— RUNNING (since <date>, s2)` by hand — the only hand edit before step 8 —
then, after `source`-ing `env.sh` and §0's extraction:

```bash
( cd "$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN" && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS )
git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | wc -l
git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD
grep -o '^### TM-[0-9]*' "$REPO/meta/DECISIONS.md" | tail -1
git -C "$WB/nitpick-regex" cat-file -e fb37391; echo "fb37391 $?"
python3 --version
free -g | sed -n 2p
journalctl -u earlyoom --since today --no-pager | grep -c -i -E "sending sig|killing"
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$REPO/harness/run.py" > "$W/run0.log" 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$CHECK/check_refs.py" "$REPO" | grep -v '^$'
```

Expected: two `OK`s; `1` — this file, just set `RUNNING` (the workbench
PLAYBOOK §12's *"N plus the plan file"*); this plan's commit; `### TM-198`;
`fb37391 0`; `Python 3.12.3`; at least 32 GiB available (the board's floor
for a dispatch); `0` kills today — a higher count is the machine's until the
journal's lines say otherwise, so read them, and a kill of anything this
subcycle started is a stop (W-12); `exit 0`; and `check_refs` clean,
`(81 md files via git ls-files; leak scan 247 text of 247 tracked)`. Then the
run's deciding lines:

```bash
{ grep "FAIL\|tree-check violations\|the tree checks  \|the verdict mechanisms\|the reader (lexical.py)\|exemption verdicts\|parse cleanly\|defect corpus\|check_denominators  \|budgeted identities\|B of IR\|^RED\|^GREEN" "$W/run0.log"; grep -o "[0-9]* unresolved, [0-9]* stale exemption(s)" "$W/run0.log"; grep -o "report: [A-Z]-[0-9]*[a-z]* cited" "$W/run0.log"; } | cut -c1-200 > "$W/step0.txt"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" step0 "$W/step0.txt"; echo "exit $?"
```

````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1 and step 2: THE PORT AGAINST ITS SOURCE (PD-50).
`nitpick-regex`'s `harness/lexical.py` and its self-check's case-18 data, at its
`fb37391`, read with `git show` in that repository and written nowhere, against
this tree's:
  (1) the two `lexical.py` syntax trees, every docstring removed -- equal, so
      the executable code is the sibling's statement for statement;
  (2) `_LEX_TEXT` and `_LEX_IMPORTS`, evaluated in both self-checks -- equal.
Before step 2 this tree has neither, and both lines say ABSENT."""
import ast, os, subprocess, sys

REPO, WB = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["WB"]
RX, AT = os.path.join(WB, "nitpick-regex"), "fb37391"


def show(path):
    p = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", RX, "show", "%s:%s" % (AT, path)],
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.PIPE, text=True)
    if p.returncode != 0:
        sys.exit("STOP: cannot read %s at %s in nitpick-regex: %s" % (path, AT, p.stderr.strip()))
    return p.stdout


def bare(src):
    t = ast.parse(src)
    for node in ast.walk(t):
        body = getattr(node, "body", None)
        if isinstance(body, list) and body and isinstance(body[0], ast.Expr) \
                and isinstance(getattr(body[0], "value", None), ast.Constant) \
                and isinstance(body[0].value.value, str):
            node.body = body[1:] or [ast.Pass()]
    return ast.dump(t)


def data(src):
    out = {}
    for node in ast.parse(src).body:
        if isinstance(node, ast.Assign) and len(node.targets) == 1 \
                and isinstance(node.targets[0], ast.Name) \
                and node.targets[0].id in ("_LEX_TEXT", "_LEX_IMPORTS"):
            out[node.targets[0].id] = ast.literal_eval(node.value)
    return out


mine = os.path.join(REPO, "harness", "lexical.py")
if os.path.isfile(mine):
    with open(mine, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        ok = bare(fh.read()) == bare(show("harness/lexical.py"))
    print("lexical.py, docstrings removed, against nitpick-regex %s: %s" % (AT, "EQUAL" if ok else "DIFFERENT"))
else:
    ok = None
    print("lexical.py, docstrings removed, against nitpick-regex %s: ABSENT" % AT)
with open(os.path.join(REPO, "harness", "selfcheck.py"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    here = data(fh.read())
there = data(show("harness/selfcheck.py"))
if not here:
    print("_LEX_TEXT and _LEX_IMPORTS against nitpick-regex %s: ABSENT" % AT)
else:
    same = here == there
    print("_LEX_TEXT and _LEX_IMPORTS against nitpick-regex %s: %s (%d lines, %d imports)"
          % (AT, "EQUAL" if same else "DIFFERENT", there["_LEX_TEXT"].count("\n"), len(there["_LEX_IMPORTS"])))
    ok = ok and same
sys.exit(0 if ok in (None, True) else 1)
````

````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1, part C of `facts.py`: each shape of §1.3 and §1.4 planted in a
scratch tree built by the harness's own `selfcheck._mini_tree`, and run
through the check it could fool. One line each: the shape, the check, what
the compiler's reading makes the check's right answer (part B measured it),
and what this harness said.

    shapes.py HARNESS_DIR"""
import os, shutil, sys, tempfile

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.abspath(sys.argv[1]))
import checks as C          # noqa: E402
import selfcheck as SC      # noqa: E402

ROWS = [
    ("P1 a lone CR in `//`, then a `\"`: a division below", C.check_literal_divisors, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\n// note\r\"\nfunc:f = int64(int64:y, int64:m) never fails { pass y / m; };\n// \"\n", "RED"),
    ("P2 `/* see // below */`, then a clock call", C.check_purity, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64() never fails { /* see // below */ pass mono_now(); };\n", "RED"),
    ("P3 `/* a // note */`, then a fourth `error:`", C.check_error_budget, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\n/* a // note */ pub error:ETimeOops;\n", "RED"),
    ("P4 `'\"'`, then a division", C.check_literal_divisors, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64(int64:y, int64:m) never fails { char8:q = '\"'; pass y / m; };\n", "RED"),
    ("P5 a template's `//`, then a division", C.check_literal_divisors, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64(int64:y, int64:m) never fails { string:t = `a//b`; pass y / m; };\n", "RED"),
    ("P6 `use \"..\\x2fhost/host.npk\"`", C.check_layering, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nuse \"..\\x2fhost/host.npk\".*;\n", "RED"),
    ("P7 `// note` CR `use \"../host/host.npk\"`: a comment", C.check_layering, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\n// note\ruse \"../host/host.npk\".*;\n", "SILENT"),
    ("P8 a `pub use` inside `/* */`, tagged 1", C.check_denominators, "src/lib.npk",
     "mod:lib;\n// " + "[[" + "sweep: lib_reexports=1" + "]]" + "\npub use \"./cal/cal.npk\".f;\n/*\npub use \"./cal/cal.npk\".g;\n*/\n",
     "SILENT"),
    ("T1 `mono_now ()`", C.check_purity, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64() never fails { pass mono_now (); };\n", "RED"),
    ("T2 `environ` LF `()`", C.check_purity, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64() never fails { pass environ\n(); };\n", "RED"),
    ("T3 `v.items` LF `[0i64]`", C.check_raw_index, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = int64(Vec:v) never fails { pass v.items\n[0i64]; };\n", "RED"),
    ("T4 `b.body.ptr [0i64]`", C.check_raw_index, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfunc:f = uint8(Bytes:b) never fails { pass b.body.ptr [0i64]; };\n", "RED"),
    ("T5 `wild int64->` LF `:p`, then `p` LF `[4i64]`", C.check_raw_index, "src/core/vec.npk",
     "mod:vec;\nfunc:f = int64(Vec:v) never fails {\n    wild int64->\n    :p = v.items;\n    pass p\n    [4i64];\n};\n", "RED"),
    ("T6 `error` LF `:ETimeOops`", C.check_error_budget, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nerror\n:ETimeOops;\n", "RED"),
    ("T7 `pub error :ETimeOops`", C.check_error_budget, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\npub error :ETimeOops;\n", "RED"),
    ("T8 a second `error:` after a `;`", C.check_error_budget, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\npub error:ETimeValue; pub error:ETimeOops;\n", "RED"),
    ("T9 `fixed` LF `int64:YEAR_MAX`", C.check_constants_named, "src/cal/cal.npk",
     "mod:cal;\nfixed\nint64:YEAR_MAX = 9999i64;\n", "RED"),
    ("T10 `struct` LF `:Row = { ... string:name; }`", C.check_no_owning_fields, "src/zone/zone.npk",
     "mod:zone;\nstruct\n:Row = { int64:id; string:name; };\npub fixed Row[2]:TABLE = [];\n", "RED"),
    ("T11 `pub fixed string` LF `[2]:NAMES`", C.check_no_owning_fields, "src/zone/zone.npk",
     "mod:zone;\npub fixed string\n[2]:NAMES = [\"a\", \"b\"];\n", "RED"),
]
base = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="shapes_")
for i, (label, fn, rel, text, want) in enumerate(ROWS):
    where = os.path.join(base, "r%d" % i)
    SC._mini_tree(where, [])
    with open(os.path.join(where, rel), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)
    res = fn(where)
    print("%s | %s | %s | %s" % (label, fn.__name__, want, "RED" if res.problems else "SILENT"))
shutil.rmtree(base)
````

````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1: EVERY FACT §1 RESTS ON, RE-DERIVED BEFORE ANYTHING CHANGES.
Builds nothing in the tree: its programs and its copy of the tree live under
$W/facts/. Its whole output is §7's `facts` block.

  A  the pin and the anchor, at both kept pins
  B  the compiler's reading of every lexical and token shape (§1.3, §1.4):
     each program through npkc, llc, ld.lld and a run, at -O0 and through
     opt -O2, at the pin and at c3bdae2
  C  the harness's reading of the same shapes, old and new: each shape planted
     in a scratch tree through the check it could fool, run once against
     HEAD's harness and once against a copy of the tree with this plan's
     `code` group applied (§4)
  D  the fifteen text-mode reads, and Python's line ends (§1.5)
  E  the arms every running file names, against NITPICK-REACH-003 (§1.6)
  F  a slice laid over `cap`: what guards each accessor's length (§1.8, PD-52)
  G  what cycle 0.1 was promised, live (§1.8)
  H  `cstring` in the tree, for the next re-pin's census (§1.10)"""
import os, re, shutil, subprocess, sys, tempfile

REPO, W, WB = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"], os.environ["WB"]
PIN, OLD = os.environ["PIN"], os.environ["OLD"]
TC = os.path.join(WB, ".internal", "toolchain")
F = os.path.join(W, "facts")
if os.path.isdir(F):
    shutil.rmtree(F)
os.makedirs(F)


def sh(argv, cwd=None, env=None):
    p = subprocess.run(argv, cwd=cwd, env=env, stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                       stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    return p.returncode, p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace")


# ---- A. the pin --------------------------------------------------------------
print("A. the pin")
for pin in (PIN, OLD):
    for obj in ("npkc", "npkrt.o"):
        p = os.path.join(TC, pin, obj)
        st, out = sh(["sha256sum", p])
        print("   %-8s %-8s %10d B  %s" % (pin, obj, os.path.getsize(p), out.split()[0]))

# ---- B. the compiler's reading ------------------------------------------------
FLOOR = """
func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest) { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)        { exit 92i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)    { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)    { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)       { exit 96i32; },
        (StackExhausted) { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)   { exit 107i32; },
        (*)              { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
"""
MAIN = "func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {\n"
PROGRAMS = [
    # (stem, text, what the harness's reader must agree with)
    ("lx_cr_comment", "int32:a = 1i32;\n    // note\r    a = 2i32;\n    exit a;\n",
     "a lone CR does not end a `//` comment: 1"),
    ("lx_block_slashes", "int32:a = 1i32;\n    /* x // y */ a = 3i32;\n    exit a;\n",
     "a `//` inside `/* */` is inert, the code after it runs: 3"),
    ("lx_block_nonnest", "int32:a = 1i32;\n    /* p /* q */ a = 4i32;\n    exit a;\n",
     "a `/* */` does not nest: 4"),
    ("lx_char_quote", "int32:a = 1i32;\n    char8:q = '\"'; a = 5i32;\n    exit a;\n",
     "`'\"'` is one character: 5"),
    ("lx_template", "int32:a = 1i32;\n    string:t = `x//y`; a = 6i32;\n    exit a;\n",
     "a template's `//` is text: 6"),
    ("lx_escaped_use", "int32:a = raw six();\n    exit a;\n",
     "an escaped `use` path is its decoded value: 6"),
    ("lx_cr_use", "exit 7i32;\n",
     "a `use` after a lone CR in a comment is comment: 7"),
    ("lx_cr_use_called", "int32:a = raw six();\n    exit a;\n",
     "... and its function is not in scope: RESOLVE-002"),
    ("lx_call_space", "int32:a = raw seven ();\n    int32:b = raw seven\n    ();\n"
     "    if (a != b) { exit 1i32; }\n    exit a;\n",
     "a call's `(` may follow a space or a line end: 7"),
    ("lx_index_space", "int64[3]:arr = [1i64, 2i64, 9i64];\n    int64:v = arr [2i64];\n"
     "    int64:w = arr\n    [2i64];\n    if (v != w) { exit 1i32; }\n    exit v =>! int32;\n",
     "an index's `[` may follow a space or a line end: 9"),
    ("ly_wild_split", "wild int8->\n    :mem = alloc(32i64);\n    dalloc(mem);\n    exit 0i32;\n",
     "a bare-pointer binding across a line end: 0"),
    ("ly_wild_index_split", "wild int8->:mem = alloc(32i64);\n    mem\n    [3i64] = 5i8;\n"
     "    int8:v = mem [3i64];\n    dalloc(mem);\n    exit v =>! int32;\n",
     "an index of it across a line end, and spaced: 5"),
    ("ly_buffer_ptr_space", "buffer:b = buffer_new(8i64);\n    b.ptr [2i64] = 9u8;\n"
     "    uint8:v = b.ptr\n    [2i64];\n    exit v =>! int32;\n",
     "a buffer's `.ptr` indexed spaced and across a line end: 9"),
]
HEADS = {
    "lx_cr_use": "// note\ruse \"./sub/lx_six.npk\".*;\n",
    "lx_cr_use_called": "// note\ruse \"./sub/lx_six.npk\".*;\n",
    "lx_escaped_use": "use \".\\x2fsub/lx_six.npk\".*;\n",
    "lx_call_space": "func:seven = int32() never fails { pass 7i32; };\n",
}
DECLS = [
    ("ly_error_colon_split", "error\n:EOops;\n", "`error` and `:EOops` on two lines: declared -- REACH-002 asks its arm"),
    ("ly_error_space", "error :EOops;\n", "`error :EOops`: declared -- REACH-002"),
    ("ly_two_errors", "error:EOne; error:EOops;\n", "a second `error:` after a `;`: declared -- REACH-002"),
    ("ly_pub_error_split", "pub\nerror:EOops;\n", "`pub` and `error:` on two lines: declared -- REACH-002"),
]
os.makedirs(os.path.join(F, "lex", "sub"))
with open(os.path.join(F, "lex", "sub", "lx_six.npk"), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
    fh.write("mod:lx_six;\npub func:six = int32() never fails { pass 6i32; };\n")
texts = []
for stem, body, why in PROGRAMS:
    texts.append((stem, "mod:%s;\n%s%s    %s};\n%s" % (stem, HEADS.get(stem, ""), MAIN, body, FLOOR), why))
for stem, decl, why in DECLS:
    texts.append((stem, "mod:%s;\n%sfunc:f = int32(int32:x) { if (x > 5i32) { fail EOops; } pass x; };\n"
                  "%s    int32:a = f(3i32) ?! EOops;\n    exit a;\n};\n%s" % (stem, decl, MAIN, FLOOR), why))
texts.append(("ly_fixed_split", "mod:ly_fixed_split;\nfixed\nint64:X_MAX = 7i64;\n%s    exit X_MAX =>! int32;\n};\n%s"
              % (MAIN, FLOOR), "`fixed` and `int64:X_MAX` on two lines: a bound -- 7"))
texts.append(("ly_table_split", "mod:ly_table_split;\nfixed int64\n[3]:T = [4i64, 5i64, 6i64];\n%s"
              "    int64:v = T[1i64];\n    exit v =>! int32;\n};\n%s" % (MAIN, FLOOR),
              "a table's `[` on the line after its type: a table -- 5"))
texts.append(("ly_struct_split", "mod:ly_struct_split;\nstruct\n:Row = { int64:id; };\n%s"
              "    Row:r = Row{ id: 3i64 };\n    exit r.id =>! int32;\n};\n%s" % (MAIN, FLOOR),
              "`struct` and `:Row` on two lines: a struct -- 3"))
texts.append(("ly_main_split", "mod:ly_main_split;\nfunc\n:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {\n    exit 8i32;\n};\n%s"
              % FLOOR, "`func` and `:main` on two lines: a main -- 8"))


def verdict(npkc, npkrt, d, stem):
    o = tempfile.mkdtemp(dir=F)
    st, out = sh([npkc, stem + ".npk", "-o", os.path.join(o, "p.ll")], cwd=d)
    if st != 0 or not os.path.isfile(os.path.join(o, "p.ll")):
        codes = sorted(set(re.findall(r"NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+", out)))
        pos = re.search(r"%s\.npk:(\d+:\d+)" % stem, out)
        return "npkc %d %s%s" % (st, " ".join(codes), (" at " + pos.group(1)) if pos else "")
    legs = []
    for leg in ("O0", "O2"):
        src = os.path.join(o, "p.ll")
        if leg == "O2":
            sh(["opt", "-O2", "-S", src, "-o", os.path.join(o, "q.ll")])
            src = os.path.join(o, "q.ll")
        sh(["llc", "-" + leg, "-filetype=obj", "-relocation-model=static", src, "-o", os.path.join(o, "p.o")])
        sh(["ld.lld", "-static", os.path.join(o, "p.o"), npkrt, "-o", os.path.join(o, "p")])
        st, _ = sh([os.path.join(o, "p")], env={"NTIME_HARNESS": "1"})
        legs.append("run:%d" % st)
    return " ".join(legs)


print("B. the compiler's reading, at %s and %s" % (PIN, OLD))
d = os.path.join(F, "lex")
for stem, text, why in texts:
    with open(os.path.join(d, stem + ".npk"), "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)
for stem, text, why in texts:
    got = [verdict(os.path.join(TC, pin, "npkc"), os.path.join(TC, pin, "npkrt.o"), d, stem)
           for pin in (PIN, OLD)]
    same = "the same at both" if got[0] == got[1] else "AT %s: %s" % (OLD, got[1])
    print("   %-22s %-38s %s" % (stem, got[0], same))
    print("   %-22s   %s" % ("", why))

# ---- C. the harness's reading, old and new ------------------------------------
print("C. the harness's reading of each shape, HEAD's harness and this plan's")
def export(dest):
    """HEAD's tree as plain files -- `git archive`, so nothing is registered in
    the repository's `.git` and nothing is written in the tree."""
    os.makedirs(dest)
    a = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "archive", "HEAD"], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, check=True)
    subprocess.run(["tar", "-x", "-C", dest], input=a.stdout, check=True)


new = os.path.join(F, "tree")
export(new)
env = dict(os.environ, REPO=new)
st, out = sh([sys.executable, "-B", os.path.join(W, "apply.py"), "code"], cwd=new, env=env)
if st != 0:
    sys.exit("STOP: the code group did not apply to the scratch copy:\n" + out)
tables = {}
for label, h in (("old", os.path.join(REPO, "harness")), ("new", os.path.join(new, "harness"))):
    st, out = sh([sys.executable, "-B", os.path.join(W, "shapes.py"), h])
    if st != 0:
        sys.exit("STOP: shapes.py against the %s harness:\n%s" % (label, out))
    tables[label] = out.splitlines()
for o, n in zip(tables["old"], tables["new"]):
    shape, check, want, got_o = o.split(" | ")
    got_n = n.split(" | ")[3]
    print("   %-54s %-24s compiler %-6s old %-6s new %s" % (shape, check, want, got_o, got_n))
old_dis = sum(1 for o in tables["old"] if o.split(" | ")[2] != o.split(" | ")[3])
new_dis = sum(1 for n in tables["new"] if n.split(" | ")[2] != n.split(" | ")[3])
print("   disagreements with the compiler: HEAD's harness %d of %d, this plan's %d"
      % (old_dis, len(tables["old"]), new_dis))

# ---- D. the fifteen text-mode reads, and Python's line ends -------------------
print("D. the harness's text-mode reads at HEAD")
# `open(` and a text mode on one line, whatever the path argument holds -- a
# nested call such as `os.path.join(tree, rel)` included.
st, out = sh(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-E", r'open\(.*"r"', "--", "harness"])
lines = sorted(out.splitlines(), key=lambda l: (l.split(":")[0], int(l.split(":")[1])))
for l in lines:
    f, n, rest = l.split(":", 2)
    print("   %s:%s" % (f, n))
print("   %d site(s)" % len(lines))
t = os.path.join(F, "crlf.txt")
with open(t, "wb") as fh:
    fh.write(b"a\rb\nc\n")
with open(t, encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    tm = fh.read()
with open(t, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
    nl = fh.read()
with open(t, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
    it = list(fh)
with open(t, encoding="utf-8", newline="\n") as fh:
    itn = list(fh)
with open(t, "rb") as fh:
    by = fh.read().decode("latin-1").split("\n")
print("   the bytes a CR b LF c LF, read five ways -- the compiler sees TWO lines:")
print("     text mode, .read()                 %r" % tm)
print("     newline='', .read().splitlines()   %r" % nl.splitlines())
print("     newline='', iterated               %r" % it)
print("     newline='\\n', iterated             %r" % itn)
print("     bytes, split at LF alone           %r" % by)

# ---- E. the arms every running file names -------------------------------------
print("E. the arms each running file's failsafe names, against NITPICK-REACH-003 at %s" % PIN)
head = os.path.join(F, "head")
export(head)
st, out = sh(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-l", "^// expect-exit:", "--", "tests/*.npk"])
files = sorted(out.split())
FS = re.compile(r"\nfunc:failsafe = int32\(Error:e\) \{.*?\n\};\n", re.S)
ARM = re.compile(r"\(((?:[a-z_]\w*\.)?[A-Z]\w*)\)\s*\{")
FIVE = ("tests/probe/probe18_zero_length_owner.npk", "tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk",
        "tests/unit/vec_churn_clear.npk", "tests/unit/vec_churn_pop.npk", "tests/unit/vec_moves.npk")
equal, over, short, extra = [], [], [], {}
for rel in files:
    src = open(os.path.join(head, rel), encoding="utf-8").read()
    m = FS.search(src)
    named = set(a.split(".")[-1] for a in ARM.findall(m.group(0)))
    stem = os.path.basename(rel)[:-4]
    body = (src[:m.start()] + "\n" + src[m.end():]).replace("mod:%s;" % stem, "mod:zz_bill_%s;" % stem, 1)
    tmp = os.path.join(head, os.path.dirname(rel), "zz_bill_%s.npk" % stem)
    with open(tmp, "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        fh.write(body)
    st, out = sh([os.path.join(TC, PIN, "npkc"), tmp, "-o", os.path.join(F, "bill.ll")], cwd=head)
    os.remove(tmp)
    lm = re.search(r"(\d+) identities: ([^\n]*?)(?: --|\n|$)", out)
    listed = set(x.strip().split(".")[-1] for x in lm.group(2).split(",")) if lm else set()
    (equal if named == listed else over if named > listed else short).append(rel)
    extra[rel] = named - listed
    if rel in FIVE:
        print("   %-44s names %2d, REACH-003 lists %2d, %s" % (rel, len(named), len(listed),
              "equal" if named == listed else "NOT equal"))
print("   every running file: %d name exactly the list, %d a superset, %d fewer -- of %d"
      % (len(equal), len(over), len(short), len(files)))
for rel in over:
    print("     a superset: %-58s +%s" % (rel, ", ".join(sorted(extra[rel]))))

# ---- F. a slice over `cap` ------------------------------------------------------
print("F. each read accessor's slice laid over `cap`, and the units that see it")
MUTS = [("vec", "src/core/vec.npk", "T[]:s = #wild_slice<T>(v.items, v.count);",
         "T[]:s = #wild_slice<T>(v.items, v.cap);",
         ["tests/unit/vec_at_past_end.npk", "tests/unit/vec_set_past_end.npk", "tests/unit/vec_pop_empty.npk"]),
        ("bytes_view", "src/core/bytes.npk", "    pass #wild_slice<uint8>(b.body.ptr, b.len);",
         "    pass #wild_slice<uint8>(b.body.ptr, b.body.cap);",
         ["tests/unit/bytes_put_int.npk", "tests/unit/bytes_growth.npk", "tests/unit/bytes_view_lifetime.npk"])]
for tag, rel, old, newtext, units in MUTS:
    c = os.path.join(F, "cap_" + tag)
    shutil.copytree(os.path.join(head, "src"), os.path.join(c, "src"))
    shutil.copytree(os.path.join(head, "tests"), os.path.join(c, "tests"))
    p = os.path.join(c, rel)
    text = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
    n = text.count(old)
    open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(text.replace(old, newtext))
    print("   %s: %d slice(s) over `cap`" % (tag, n))
    for u in units:
        want = re.search(r"^// expect-exit: (\d+)", open(os.path.join(head, u), encoding="utf-8").read(), re.M).group(1)
        got = verdict(os.path.join(TC, PIN, "npkc"), os.path.join(TC, PIN, "npkrt.o"),
                      os.path.dirname(os.path.join(c, u)), os.path.basename(u)[:-4])
        print("     %-38s asserts %-3s  over cap: %s" % (u, want, got))

# ---- G. what cycle 0.1 was promised -------------------------------------------
print("G. live statements that cycle 0.1 would do something")
st, out = sh(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-I", "-E",
              r"is cycle 0\.1's|Cycle 0\.1\*\*, with|deferred to (cycle )?0\.1|"
              r"generated at cycle 0\.1|before cycle 0\.1|in cycle 0\.1 needs",
              "--", ".", ":!meta/roadmap/done/*", ":!meta/roadmap/0.1/*", ":!*TRANSCRIPT.txt"])
for l in sorted(out.splitlines()):
    f, n, text = l.split(":", 2)
    print("   %s:%s: %s" % (f, n, re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text.strip())[:88]))
# ---- H. `cstring` in the tree ---------------------------------------------------
print("H. `cstring` in the tree, for the next re-pin's census")


def grep_n(args):
    st, out = sh(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n"] + args)
    return [l for l in out.splitlines() if l]


MAIN_ARGV = re.compile(r"func:main = int32\(cstring\[\]:")
mains = grep_n(["-E", MAIN_ARGV.pattern, "--", "tests/*.npk"])
print("   test files' `main` taking `cstring[]`: %d line(s)" % len(mains))
for l in grep_n(["-F", "cstring[]", "--", "tests/*.npk"]):
    if not MAIN_ARGV.search(l):
        print("   `cstring[]` besides a `main`: %s" % ":".join(l.split(":", 2)[:2]))
per = {}
for l in grep_n(["-w", "to_cstring", "--", "tests/*.npk"]):
    per[l.split(":", 1)[0]] = per.get(l.split(":", 1)[0], 0) + 1
print("   `to_cstring` in tests: %d line(s) in %d file(s)" % (sum(per.values()), len(per)))
for f in sorted(per):
    print("     %-48s %d" % (f, per[f]))
heap = sorted({l.split(":", 1)[0] for l in grep_n(["-E", "^// heap:", "--", "tests/*.npk"])})
print("   files bounded by a `heap:` marker: %d, of which call `to_cstring`: %d"
      % (len(heap), sum(1 for f in heap if f in per)))
for l in grep_n(["-w", "-E", "cstring|to_cstring", "--", "harness"]):
    print("   harness: %s" % ":".join(l.split(":", 2)[:2]))
print("facts: done")
````

<!-- script: sweep.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 1 (`before`) and step 5 (`after`): every `.npk` in the tree --
the tracked ones and any new one not yet added -- rooted at $NPKC from its own
directory: its exit and the first position of each code it reports. `before`
saves $W/sweep_before.txt; `after` saves $W/sweep_after.txt and compares the
two file by file. The close edits the COMMENTS of eleven `.npk` files and no
code, so the claim this checks is that no file's verdict or position moved --
one of them, `missing_failsafe/case1`, asserts its refusal at `62:1`, which a
comment edit that added a line would have moved. And `after` reads every
`.npk` file's CODE -- its text with comments and literals blanked by the
reader step 2 added, split into tokens -- against HEAD's: equal in every file
means no statement was added, and so no `for`, `loop` or `till`, whose shapes
at a type's edge the compiler counts wrong at this pin.

    sweep.py before | after"""
import os, re, subprocess, sys

REPO, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"]
DIAG = re.compile(rb"^(NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+) (\S+?):(\d+):(\d+):", re.M)
mode = sys.argv[1]
files = sorted(set(subprocess.run(
    ["git", "-C", REPO, "ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard", "*.npk"],
    stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True, check=True).stdout.split()))


def line(rel):
    d, b = os.path.split(os.path.join(REPO, rel))
    p = subprocess.run([os.environ["NPKC"], b, "-o", "/dev/null"], cwd=d,
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    seen, got = set(), []
    for m in DIAG.finditer(p.stdout):
        code, path, ln, col = (x.decode() for x in m.groups())
        if code not in seen:
            seen.add(code)
            where = "" if os.path.basename(path) == b else os.path.basename(path) + ":"
            got.append("%s@%s%s:%s" % (code, where, ln, col))
    return "%-62s exit %d %s" % (rel, p.returncode, " ".join(got))


now = {rel: line(rel) for rel in files}
with open(os.path.join(W, "sweep_%s.txt" % mode), "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    fh.write("\n".join(now[r] for r in files) + "\n")
ok = sum(1 for r in files if " exit 0 " in now[r] + " ")
print("%s: %d file(s), %d compile, %d refused" % (mode, len(files), ok, len(files) - ok))
if mode == "after":
    before = {}
    with open(os.path.join(W, "sweep_before.txt"), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        for l in fh.read().splitlines():
            before[l.split()[0]] = l
    new = [r for r in files if r not in before]
    gone = [r for r in before if r not in now]
    changed = [r for r in files if r in before and before[r].rstrip() != now[r].rstrip()]
    print("against before: %d new, %d gone, %d changed, %d the same"
          % (len(new), len(gone), len(changed), len(files) - len(new) - len(changed)))
    for r in changed:
        print("  changed %s\n      before %s\n      after  %s" % (r, before[r].split(" exit ", 1)[1],
                                                                 now[r].split(" exit ", 1)[1]))
    for r in new + gone:
        print("  %s %s" % ("new " if r in new else "GONE", r))
    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(REPO, "harness"))
    import lexical                                   # noqa: E402 -- step 2's
    LOOP = re.compile(r"\b(for|loop|till)\b")
    moved, loops_then, loops_now = [], 0, 0
    for rel in files:
        was = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "show", "HEAD:" + rel],
                             stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
        now_code = lexical.blank(lexical.read(os.path.join(REPO, rel)))
        loops_now += len(LOOP.findall(now_code))
        if was.returncode != 0:
            moved.append(rel + " (not at HEAD)")
            continue
        then_code = lexical.blank(was.stdout.decode("latin-1"))
        loops_then += len(LOOP.findall(then_code))
        if then_code.split() != now_code.split():
            moved.append(rel)
    print("code against HEAD, comments and literals blanked: %d file(s) differ%s"
          % (len(moved), "".join("\n  CODE MOVED " + r for r in moved)))
    print("`for`, `loop` and `till` in code: %d at HEAD, %d now" % (loops_then, loops_now))
````

**Step 5 — the full run, what moved in it, the mutants, the per-file sweep.**

<!-- script: mut.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 5: THE MUTATION MATRIX (`0.1.5.md` §7 step 5). Every new
self-check row and part must go red when the thing it guards is taken away.
Each mutant is ONE substitution -- or, for M6, two -- in a scratch copy of the
applied tree under $W/mut/, and the whole runner is run there: the self-check
runs first and its failure is fatal, so what comes back is the self-check's
red, summarised by what failed. The applied tree itself is never touched.

  M1   the reader in text mode              (the read, V-1k)
  M2   the old comment scanner              (`strip_comments`)
  M2b  the old string blanker               (`blank_code`)
  M3   an import path not decoded           (`lexical.imports`)
  M3b  the re-exports counted by line       (`denominators`)
  M4a  a pattern's whitespace without LF    (V-1l)
  M4b  the purity call as its old spelling  (`check_purity`)
  M4c  a declaration only at a line's start (`check_error_budget`)
  M4d  a main found by its substring        (`run._verdict`)
  M5   a form the compiler reads otherwise  (part E's compiler half)
  M6   M1 and M2b together                  (the old reader whole)"""
import collections, os, re, shutil, subprocess, sys

REPO, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"]
OLD_BLANK = r'''    out, i, n, s = [], 0, len(text), False
    while i < n:
        c = text[i]
        if s:
            if c == "\\" and i + 1 < n:
                out.append(text[i:i + 2]); i += 2; continue
            if c == '"':
                s = False
            out.append(c); i += 1; continue
        if c == '"':
            s = True; out.append(c); i += 1; continue
        if c == "/" and i + 1 < n and text[i + 1] == "/":
            j = text.find("\n", i); j = n if j < 0 else j
            out.append(" " * (j - i)); i = j; continue
        out.append(c); i += 1
    return "".join(out)
'''
OLD_STRINGS = OLD_BLANK.replace('    return "".join(out)\n', '    text = "".join(out)\n') + r'''    out, i, n, s = [], 0, len(text), False
    while i < n:
        c = text[i]
        if s:
            if c == "\\" and i + 1 < n:
                out.append("  "); i += 2; continue
            if c == '"':
                s = False; out.append(c)
            else:
                out.append(" " if c != "\n" else "\n")
            i += 1; continue
        if c == '"':
            s = True
        out.append(c); i += 1
    return "".join(out)
'''
SUBS = {
    "M1": [("harness/lexical.py",
            '    with open(path, "rb") as fh:\n        return fh.read().decode("latin-1")\n',
            '    with open(path, "r", encoding="latin-1") as fh:\n        return fh.read()\n')],
    "M2": [("harness/checks.py",
            '    return lexical.blank(text, [s for s in lexical.spans(text)\n'
            '                                if s[0] == "comment"])\n', OLD_BLANK)],
    "M2b": [("harness/checks.py", "    return lexical.blank(text)\n", OLD_STRINGS)],
    "M3": [("harness/lexical.py",
            "        target = os.fsdecode(_decode(text[j + 1:plain[j] - 1]))\n",
            "        target = text[j + 1:plain[j] - 1]\n")],
    "M3b": [("harness/checks.py",
             '        d["lib_reexports"] = sum(\n'
             '            1 for _, _, pub in lexical.imports(lexical.read(lib)) if pub)\n',
             '        d["lib_reexports"] = sum(\n'
             '            1 for l in lexical.read(lib).split("\\n") if l.startswith("pub use "))\n')],
    "M4a": [("harness/checks.py", '_W = r"[ \\t\\r\\n]"\n', '_W = r"[ \\t]"\n')],
    "M4b": [("harness/checks.py",
             '    "(%s)%s*\\\\(" % ("|".join(re.escape(b[:-1]) for b in PURITY_BAN), _W))\n',
             '    "(%s)\\\\(" % "|".join(re.escape(b[:-1]) for b in PURITY_BAN))\n')],
    "M4c": [("harness/checks.py",
             '_ERROR_DECL = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.])(pub%s+)?error%s*:%s*"\n',
             '_ERROR_DECL = re.compile(r"(?m)^[ \\t]*(pub%s+)?error%s*:%s*"\n')],
    "M4d": [("harness/run.py", "    if not _MAIN.search(text):\n",
             '    if "func:main" not in text:\n')],
    "M5": [("harness/selfcheck.py",
            '    "    if (a != 0i32) { exit 10i32; }\\n"                     # 7\n',
            '    "    if (a == 0i32) { exit 10i32; }\\n"                     # 7\n')],
}
SUBS["M6"] = SUBS["M1"] + SUBS["M2b"]
WHAT = {"M1": "the reader in text mode", "M2": "the old comment scanner",
        "M2b": "the old string blanker", "M3": "an import path not decoded",
        "M3b": "the re-exports counted by line", "M4a": "a pattern's whitespace without LF",
        "M4b": "the purity call as its old spelling", "M4c": "a declaration only at a line's start",
        "M4d": "a main found by its substring", "M5": "a form the compiler reads otherwise",
        "M6": "M1 and M2b together"}
PAT = re.compile(r"(check_\w+|run\._verdict) (did not fire on a planted violation in|"
                 r"fired on the CLEAN control in|fired on the plant in|said) (\S+)")
KIND = {"did not fire on a planted violation in": "silent on its plant in",
        "fired on the CLEAN control in": "fired on its control in",
        "fired on the plant in": "fired, wrong words, in", "said": "said"}
base = os.path.join(W, "mut")
if os.path.isdir(base):
    shutil.rmtree(base)
os.makedirs(base)
problems = 0
for tag in ("M1", "M2", "M2b", "M3", "M3b", "M4a", "M4b", "M4c", "M4d", "M5", "M6"):
    w = os.path.join(base, tag)
    shutil.copytree(REPO, w, ignore=shutil.ignore_patterns(".git", "build", ".internal", "__pycache__"))
    for rel, old, new in SUBS[tag]:
        p = os.path.join(w, rel)
        with open(p, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
            text = fh.read()
        if text.count(old) != 1:
            sys.exit("STOP: %s: the substitution occurs %d times in %s" % (tag, text.count(old), rel))
        with open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
            fh.write(text.replace(old, new))
    r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", "harness/run.py"], cwd=w,
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, text=True)
    red = collections.Counter()
    for line in r.stdout.splitlines():
        if line.startswith("  ok "):
            continue                      # a part that passed prints its own E1/E2 words
        m = PAT.search(line)
        if m:
            red["%s %s %s" % (m.group(1), KIND[m.group(2)], m.group(3).rstrip(".,"))] += 1
        m2 = re.search(r"\b(E[12]): (line \d+|the [^.:]{0,40})", line)
        if m2:
            red["part E %s: %s" % (m2.group(1), m2.group(2))] += 1
    print("%-4s %-38s exit %d" % (tag, WHAT[tag], r.returncode))
    for k in sorted(red):
        print("       %-66s x%d" % (k, red[k]))
    if r.returncode == 0 or not red:
        problems += 1
        print("       STOP: the mutant was not caught by any named line")
    shutil.rmtree(w)
print("mutants: %d, each red by a named line: %s" % (len(SUBS), "yes" if not problems else "NO"))
sys.exit(1 if problems else 0)
````

```bash
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$REPO/harness/run.py" > "$W/run5.log" 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
{ grep "FAIL\|tree-check violations\|the tree checks  \|the verdict mechanisms\|the reader (lexical.py)\|exemption verdicts\|parse cleanly\|defect corpus\|check_denominators  \|budgeted identities\|B of IR\|^RED\|^GREEN" "$W/run5.log"; grep -o "[0-9]* unresolved, [0-9]* stale exemption(s)" "$W/run5.log"; grep "src/lib.npk owes\|src/cal/cal.npk owes\|src/core/core.npk owes" "$W/run5.log"; } | cut -c1-200 > "$W/final.txt"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" final "$W/final.txt"; echo "exit $?"
M='s/[0-9]+\.[0-9]+ s\b/N.N s/g; s/[0-9]+ citation\(s\) over/N citation(s) over/; s/(report: [A-Z]-[0-9]+[a-z]* cited).*/\1/'
diff <(sed -E "$M" "$W/run0.log") <(sed -E "$M" "$W/run5.log") > "$W/rundiff.txt"; echo "diff exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" rundiff "$W/rundiff.txt"; echo "exit $?"
free -g | sed -n 2p
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/mut.py" > "$W/mut.txt" 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" mut "$W/mut.txt"; echo "exit $?"
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/sweep.py" after > "$W/sweep.txt"; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" sweep "$W/sweep.txt"; echo "exit $?"
```

**Step 7 — CI.**

<!-- script: ci_read.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5 step 7: CI's job log, read by script rather than by eye. `gh run view
--log` can print nothing for a run whose log exists (the workbench PLAYBOOK
§6), so the log is fetched through the jobs API into a file, and this prints
the lines that decide the run -- each GitHub timestamp removed, and CI's own
`npkc` digest, which is reported and never asserted, reduced to whether it is
there -- for step 7 to compare with its block.

    ci_read.py LOGFILE"""
import re, sys

with open(sys.argv[1], "rb") as fh:
    text = fh.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
lines = [re.sub(r"^\S+Z ", "", l).rstrip() for l in text.splitlines()]
WANT = (r"^compiler HEAD == ", r"^llvm-config --version == ", r"^npkc   WB: ",
        r"^npkrt  CI: ", r"^npkrt  WB: ", r"^built from compiler ",
        r"then \d+ tree-check violations", r"the tree checks  ", r"the verdict mechanisms  ",
        r"the reader \(lexical\.py\)", r"budgeted identities are not declared yet",
        r"exemption verdicts: ", r"defect corpus: ", r"parse cleanly, ",
        r"^GREEN -- ", r"^RED -- ")
for l in lines:
    if any(re.search(rx, l) for rx in WANT):
        print(l.strip())
ci = [l for l in lines if l.startswith("npkc   CI: ")]
print("npkc   CI: %s" % ("reported, one line" if len(ci) == 1 else "%d line(s)" % len(ci)))
print("unqualified GREEN lines: %d" % sum(1 for l in lines if l.startswith("GREEN -- ")))
````

```bash
gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/runs/<id>/jobs --jq '.jobs[0].id'
gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/jobs/<job>/logs > "$W/ci.log"; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/ci_read.py" "$W/ci.log" > "$W/ci.txt"; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" ci "$W/ci.txt"; echo "exit $?"
```
