**This block is a prediction** — CI cannot be rehearsed — from the workflow's
own `echo` lines, 0.1.3c's CI log at the same pin, and step 5's local run; the
pin does not move, so the compiler is a cache hit. **A CI line that differs is
a finding to record, not a failure to explain away.**

**Step 8 — the record, and `READY-TO-CLOSE`.** The record commit —
`cycle 0.1.5: the first half's record, READY-TO-CLOSE — CI run <id> at c970483`:

1. **This file's title**:
   `# 0.1.5 — the close of cycle 0.1 — DONE (<date>, the first half; the CYCLE is not closed — READY-TO-CLOSE, the audit next)`.
   `check_record` accepts a title of `PLANNED`, `RUNNING (…)` or `DONE (…)`
   only, and reads `READY-TO-CLOSE` as done-like, so the title says `DONE`
   and its parenthesis says what is not — as `nitpick-regex`'s close titled
   itself at its `READY-TO-CLOSE`s.
2. **§10's first-half boxes ticked**, each with its evidence; the second
   half's left.
3. **The execution record**, appended under `## Execution record` below:
   what was done, step by step, each block's `same.py` line; what was found;
   what it cost; and three sections a close owes (0.0.6's model):
   **the cycle's findings** as a numbered list — everything cycle 0.1 found
   that was not a planned deliverable, each with its subcycle and its
   decision, starting from §12.4's list; **what cycle 0.1 taught** — the
   cycle's most durable output, starting from §12.5's draft, each lesson with
   the subcycle that paid for it; and **the calendar certified**: §1.8's
   trace re-read, rule by rule.
4. **The REPORT block** as the record's last entry: `REPORT nitpick-time
   0.1.5`, `status: READY-TO-CLOSE`, every key the worker skill's §9 names,
   `next:` the cycle audit.
5. **The cycle README**: in the 0.1.5 row and in its checklist section's
   heading, *"PLANNED 2026-09-26"* becomes *"READY-TO-CLOSE <date>, the audit
   next"*, and the section's first five items are ticked, each with its
   evidence. **`ROADMAP.md`'s 0.1 row**: its closing clause — *next 0.1.5, the
   close*, the link to this file, and *planned* — becomes *0.1.5, the close:
   its first half done, READY-TO-CLOSE, the audit next*, the link kept.

Then `check_refs` clean, `check_record "$REPO" 0.1.5` **clean**, commit, push,
and CI on the record commit read after its push and named in the REPORT as
not in the record (the commit that records a CI run cannot contain its own).

---

## 8. The second half — after the audit

**Planned as far as it can be before the audit exists.** The re-dispatch
carries `AUDIT:` naming the report the orchestrator filed. **The audit's
findings are not known here, so their fixes are not planned here**; what is
planned is the order, the gate, the triage's form, the branch on the
verdict, the archive, the opening of cycle 0.2 and the close's texts.

### 8.1 Gate for starting the second half

1. `AUDIT:` names `meta/audits/nitpick-time-0.1-<date>.md` in the workbench,
   and the file exists. It states its verdict near its top — `status: ACCEPT`
   or `status: DO-NOT-ACCEPT`, as each of `nitpick-regex`'s filed audits did;
   if it states none, ask the orchestrator before triaging. **Its sha256** —
   `sha256sum "$WB/meta/audits/<file>"` — goes into the record, which names
   the audit by path and digest and does not copy it (0.0.6 did the same).
2. `$WB/BOARD.md` shows `nitpick-time` `CLAIMED s2`; the tree is clean; `HEAD`
   is the first half's record commit or a descendant of it the orchestrator
   made. **The pin is `c970483`**: the dispatch's `TOOLCHAIN` names it, and
   `sha256sum -c` passes. **If the dispatch names another pin, stop with
   `BLOCKED`** — the adoption is its own subcycle (§12.3), and a close that
   adopts a pin inside itself is two things at once.
3. `env.sh` still resolves; **re-extract the scripts** from this file into
   `$W` (§0) — `apply.py`, `same.py`, `stale.py`, `reasons.py`, `sweep.py` and
   `archive.py` are the second half's.
4. **`free -g`, `journalctl -u earlyoom`**, as at step 0.

### 8.2 The triage — every finding, one row each

**Every finding gets exactly one row in the record's triage table, in the
audit's order, and none is dropped or merged silently** (W-22):

| Finding | Severity | Verdict | Where it went |
|---|---|---|---|
| the audit's id | its severity (contradiction, unverified claim, dormant, stale, cosmetic) | **FIXED** — what changed, and the check or run that shows it; **DECLINED** — the reason, and the measurement if the finding is a claim about the tree; or **DEFERRED** — the cycle, the reason, and where the deferral is written so it cannot be lost | the commit, the file and line, or the checklist item |

The rules the triage keeps: **a finding about the compiler** is recorded as a
question to the compiler (`meta/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`, and the orchestrator
raises it), never an edit in `../nitpick`; **a finding that needs a decision**
takes the next TM number, with its alternatives, in the triage commit; **a
text fix is swept** — `stale.py` and `reasons.py` re-run after the fixes,
their outputs compared with step 4's blocks, and every new line read, since
a fix that restates something is a restatement too; **a harness change is
planted** — a self-check row seen red on its violation and silent on its
control before it is committed; and **a deferral to cycle 0.2** is written
into cycle 0.2's README (§8.5), not only into the record.

Then the full run, `check_refs`, commit 3 —
`cycle 0.1.5: the audit's findings — <the three that matter most, in words>`
— push, and CI read by `ci_read.py`.

### 8.3 The branch on the verdict

- **`status: DO-NOT-ACCEPT`** — commit 3 is the dispatch's only commit. The
  record gains the triage and a new REPORT, `status: READY-TO-CLOSE`, `next:`
  the re-audit; the title's parenthesis names the audit pass. **Nothing is
  archived**: a folder in `done/` is a claim that the cycle is finished
  (`nitpick-regex`'s RX-140, which archived at its first attempt and reversed
  it). The orchestrator re-audits, and this section runs again with the new
  report.
- **`status: ACCEPT`** — the findings the audit still raised are triaged the
  same way (an accepting audit can carry findings), commit 3 lands if any
  changed the tree, and then §8.4 to §8.7, as commit 4.

### 8.4 The archive — `archive.py`

**`git mv meta/roadmap/0.1 meta/roadmap/done/0.1`, and what navigates to it
re-pointed** — by the line RX-140 drew and this repository's 0.0.6 followed:
**live navigation is corrected; historical claims are not.** Inside the
archive, a markdown link that leaves the folder gains one `../`; a plain path
inside it is left, because it says where a file was when its note was written.
Outside it, every mention of the folder — a link or a plain path, in any
tracked text file — is re-pointed, except in the records: `DECISIONS.md`'s
settled text, the earlier archive `done/0.0/`, and the transcripts.

<!-- script: archive.py -->
````python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.5's second half (`0.1.5.md` §8.4): ARCHIVE CYCLE 0.1 -- `git mv
meta/roadmap/0.1 meta/roadmap/done/0.1` -- and re-point what navigates to it,
by the line `nitpick-regex`'s RX-140 drew and this repository's 0.0.6 followed:
LIVE NAVIGATION IS CORRECTED, HISTORICAL CLAIMS ARE NOT.

  * INSIDE the archive, markdown LINKS only: a link that leaves the folder
    gains one `../`, because the folder moved one level down. A plain path
    mention inside the archive is left: it says where a file was when its note
    was written.
  * OUTSIDE it, every mention of the folder -- a link or a plain path, in any
    tracked text file -- is re-pointed to `done/0.1/`, EXCEPT in the records:
    `meta/DECISIONS.md` (a settled decision's text is never rewritten), the
    earlier archive `meta/roadmap/done/0.0/`, and the transcripts.

    archive.py check   -- report what would move; move and write nothing
    archive.py apply   -- move, rewrite, and report

Prints every file it rewrote with its count; stops if the folder is already
gone, if `done/0.1` exists, or if a live mention is left after the rewrite.
Reads and writes with `newline=""`, so no byte it was not asked to change moves."""
import os, re, subprocess, sys

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
OLD, NEW = "meta/roadmap/0.1", "meta/roadmap/done/0.1"
RECORDS = re.compile(r"^meta/DECISIONS\.md$|^meta/roadmap/done/0\.0/|TRANSCRIPT\.txt$|^meta/roadmap/0\.1/")
# A mention of the folder: a repository path (`meta/roadmap/0.1/`, and the
# relative `roadmap/0.1/` the specifications use), or a sibling-relative one
# (`../0.1/`, and `0.1/` from `ROADMAP.md`) not preceded by a path character.
MENTION = re.compile(r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9_./-])((?:\.\./)*(?:meta/)?roadmap/)0\.1/"
                     r"|(?<![A-Za-z0-9_./-])((?:\.\./)+)0\.1/"
                     r"|(?<![A-Za-z0-9_./-])0\.1/(?=0\.1\.|README\.md|decreases_read)")
LINK = re.compile(r"\]\(([^)#\s]+)(#[^)]*)?\)")


def read(p):
    with open(p, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def write(p, t):
    with open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(t)


def tracked():
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "ls-files"], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout
    return [l for l in out.split("\n") if l]


def repoint_outside(text):
    def sub(m):
        if m.group(1) is not None:                  # `…roadmap/0.1/`
            return m.group(1) + "done/0.1/"
        if m.group(2) is not None:                  # `../0.1/`
            return m.group(2) + "done/0.1/"
        return "done/0.1/"                          # `0.1/` beside `done/`
    return MENTION.subn(sub, text)


def repoint_inside(rel_new, text):
    """`rel_new` is the file's NEW path; a link resolved from its OLD directory
    that lands outside the folder gains one `../`."""
    old_dir = os.path.dirname(rel_new.replace(NEW, OLD, 1))
    count = 0

    def sub(m):
        nonlocal count
        t = m.group(1)
        if t.startswith(("http://", "https://", "mailto:", "#", "/")):
            return m.group(0)
        landed = os.path.normpath(os.path.join(old_dir, t))
        if landed == OLD or landed.startswith(OLD + "/"):
            return m.group(0)                       # stays inside the folder
        count += 1
        # `]` and `(` built apart: the plan quotes this script, and a link's
        # two characters together inside it would be read as a link.
        return "]" + "(" + "../" + t + (m.group(2) or "") + ")"
    return LINK.sub(sub, text), count


mode = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "check"
if mode not in ("check", "apply"):
    sys.exit("usage: archive.py check|apply")
if not os.path.isdir(os.path.join(REPO, OLD)):
    sys.exit("STOP: %s is not a directory -- already archived?" % OLD)
if os.path.exists(os.path.join(REPO, NEW)):
    sys.exit("STOP: %s exists" % NEW)
files = tracked()
inside = [f for f in files if f.startswith(OLD + "/")]
print("the folder: %d tracked file(s)" % len(inside))
out_n, out_f = 0, 0
for rel in files:
    if rel.startswith(OLD + "/") or RECORDS.search(rel):
        continue
    p = os.path.join(REPO, rel)
    try:
        text = read(p)
    except (UnicodeDecodeError, IsADirectoryError):
        continue
    new, n = repoint_outside(text)
    if n:
        out_n += n
        out_f += 1
        print("  outside  %-58s %3d mention(s)" % (rel, n))
        if mode == "apply":
            write(p, new)
print("outside the folder: %d mention(s) in %d live file(s)" % (out_n, out_f))
left = sum(len(MENTION.findall(read(os.path.join(REPO, f)))) for f in files
           if f.endswith(".md") and f.startswith("meta/DECISIONS"))
print("left as written in meta/DECISIONS.md, settled text: %d mention(s)" % left)
if mode == "apply":
    r = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "mv", OLD, NEW])
    if r.returncode != 0:
        sys.exit("STOP: git mv exited %d" % r.returncode)
in_n = 0
for rel in inside:
    rel_new = rel.replace(OLD, NEW, 1)
    p = os.path.join(REPO, rel_new if mode == "apply" else rel)
    if not rel.endswith(".md"):
        continue
    new, n = repoint_inside(rel_new, read(p))
    if n:
        in_n += n
        print("  inside   %-58s %3d link(s)" % (rel_new, n))
        if mode == "apply":
            write(p, new)
print("inside the folder: %d link(s) re-pointed one level deeper" % in_n)
if mode == "apply":
    stale = []
    for rel in tracked():
        if rel.startswith(NEW + "/") or RECORDS.search(rel):
            continue
        try:
            if MENTION.search(read(os.path.join(REPO, rel))):
                stale.append(rel)
        except (UnicodeDecodeError, IsADirectoryError, FileNotFoundError):
            pass
    if stale:
        sys.exit("STOP: live mentions left in: %s" % ", ".join(stale))
    print("archived: %s -> %s; no live mention left" % (OLD, NEW))
````

```bash
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/archive.py" check > "$W/archive_check.txt"; echo "exit $?"
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/archive.py" apply > "$W/archive.txt"; echo "exit $?"
git -C "$REPO" status --short | grep -c '^R'
python3 -B "$CHECK/check_refs.py" "$REPO" | grep -v '^$'
```

## 9. What will bite

- **A lone CR is lost before any scan runs.** Anything that writes a
  planted CR must write bytes (`_write`'s `newline=""`, part E's `"wb"`); a
  fixture written in text mode on another platform would not contain the byte
  the row is about. `lexical.read` is the only way in.
- **`splitlines()` splits at a CR even on text read with `newline=""`.**
  Every caller of the reader splits at `"\n"`; a later edit that "tidies" one
  back to `splitlines()` reopens P1 and P7 — and part E's E1 is what goes red.
- **`blank_code` blanks literals; `strip_comments` does not.** A check that
  must not read a string calls `blank_code`; the error budget does, so
  `"error:X"` in a literal is not a declaration.
- **The token patterns have no word boundary before a banned name**, exactly
  as the old substrings had none: `reopen(` matches `open(` as it always did.
  PD-51 widened the whitespace, not the names; a narrower name match is a
  separate decision.
- **`lexical.py` is `nitpick-regex`'s code.** An "improvement" to it here is a
  divergence from the sibling that only a comparison like `lexsame.py`'s
  finds, and part E may not catch. A change to the reader is made in both
  libraries or in neither, and the ecosystem audit is told (§12.2).
- **`renumber.py` renumbers the cycle README too**: this plan's note and
  checklist there name PD-50 … PD-52, and step 3's block expects their nine
  labels to become the numbers.
- **The citation count in the run moves with every document edit, this file
  included** — which is why step 0 and step 5 compare the unresolved count
  and `rundiff` sets the total aside. A worker who "fixes" a block by copying
  the count has compared nothing.
- **`check_record` wants `DONE (…)` in the title at `READY-TO-CLOSE`** (§7
  step 8), and a REPORT is never edited once committed (W-28): the second
  half appends a new one.
- **The archive moves this file.** Every script that reads `$PLAN` must be
  pointed at `done/0.1/0.1.5.md` after §8.4; `check_record` finds it alone.
- **No `for`, `loop` or `till`** — in a fix the audit asks for as much as in
  anything else, while the pin is `c970483`. `sweep.py after` counts them.

### 12.5 What cycle 0.1 taught — a draft for the record

1. **Measure the premise before building on it** — 0.1.3b's check and
   0.1.4b's instrument were each resting on a sentence nobody had re-measured.
2. **An adoption is measured on the unchanged tree first, at both pins** —
   0.1.0b and 0.1.4c each found what moved before anything was changed, and
   each landing became a regression test with its old verdict as the control.
3. **The exhaustive gate costs seconds** — 22.5 s for six members, every day
   of the range both ways and every date of years 1 … 9999 against Python.
4. **A check is only as good as its reading of the source** — and the reading
   that matters is the compiler's (this close).
5. **Two passes, by id and by shape**, find what one misses (0.1.3c, and this
   close's five present tenses).
6. **The pages read first are corrected last** — a summary page cites no
   rule, so no citation sweep reaches it; read them whole at every close.
7. **A plan rehearsed from its own blocks catches its own defects** — every
   subcycle from 0.1.2 on found at least one at rehearsal.

## 13. How this plan was rehearsed — which command ran where

**Four passes in fresh clones beside a linked workbench, then one in the
REAL checkout on this plan's commit.** Each clone was `git clone` of the real
repository at `36c038f`, beside a workbench of symbolic links — `BOARD.md`,
`PLAYBOOK.md`, `README.md`, `WORKSTREAMS.md`, `skills/`,
`.internal/toolchain/` and `nitpick-regex` — and the compiler as `../nitpick`,
so `env.sh` resolved `$WB`, `$CHECK`, both toolchains and the sibling as it
does in the real position, and nothing was written in the workbench, in
`nitpick-regex` or in the compiler. Each clone carried this plan's commit as a
scratch commit of the same three files, so `HEAD` was the plan's commit, as
the gate asks. **A driver extracted this file's `bash` blocks and ran them in
order** — §0's two, then steps 0 to 6 — with the title set `RUNNING` by hand
before step 0, as step 0 says; every compared block went through `same.py`,
and step 6's `git add -A` staged in the clone and nothing was committed.
**Not rehearsed:** step 6's commit and push; step 7, whose block is a
prediction; step 8's record; and the second half, but for §8.4's archive —
run on pass 2's clone after its step 6, the applied state committed there as
a scratch commit, and the full run repeated on the archived tree.

| Pass | Where, when | The text | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | clone, 13:29–13:42 | the first build | every block ran to its end; its outputs became the first expected blocks; findings 1 to 3 below |
| 2 | clone, 13:46–13:59 | findings 1 and 2 corrected | every compared block `0 difference(s)` but `facts` (part D's two new sites) and `sweep` (`0 changed`), the two corrections; then §8.4's archive on its clone — 14 files moved, 98 mentions in 32 files, `check_refs` clean, and the run `GREEN -- 112`, equal to step 5's line for line |
| 3 | clone, 14:03–14:16 | §1's prose and the drafted decisions corrected; `facts.py` part E printing each superset's extra arms | every compared block `0 difference(s)` but `facts`' thirteen superset lines — the new column |
| 4 | clone, 14:25–14:38 | this file's blocks | **every compared block `0 difference(s)`** — `step0` 13 lines, `facts` 159, `step1` 5, `stale_before` 150, `reasons_before` 53, `step2` 52, `step3` 16, `stale_after` 138, `reasons_after` 52, `final` 15, `rundiff` 27, `mut` 54, `sweep` 4, `step6` 38; `check_refs` clean at 81 and 247, then 81 and 248; `check_record`'s one finding `[no-report]` |
| real | `$REPO`, 14:47–15:00 | the same blocks, on this plan's commit | **every compared block `0 difference(s)`**, the same fourteen; step 0 `GREEN -- 112` in 128.8 s and step 5 `GREEN -- 112` in 130.9 s; `check_refs` clean at 81 and 247 before staging and at 81 and 248 after; `check_record`'s one finding the expected `[no-report]` |

**Found by the passes and corrected above** — each a defect of the plan's
text, none of the work it plans:

1. **`facts.py` part D counted 13 text-mode reads, not 15.** Its pattern,
   `open\([^)]*"r"`, stopped at the `)` of a nested call —
   `open(os.path.join(tree, rel), "r", …)` — so `checks.py`'s two whole-tree
   scanners were missed. The pattern is now `open\(.*"r"`, and the sites print
   in file-and-line order.
2. **`probe11c`'s comment edit moved the compiler's reported position of its
   refusal from 60:5 to 61:5** — one line longer — and `sweep.py after` said
   so (`1 changed`). The file asserts only its code, so the run stayed green,
   and §1.2 claims no reported position moves: the edit is line for line
   now, as `missing_failsafe/case1`'s always was.
3. **`renumber.py` renumbers the cycle README's labels too** — nine, in the
   note and checklist this plan's commit writes there. Not a defect, since the
   README should cite the numbers once they exist, but a surprise for a
   worker reading step 3's block: §9 names it.

**The real checkout was put back to this plan's commit afterwards, and shown
to be**: `git reset --hard` to it; `$W` removed through `"${W:?}"`; and
`build/`, `.internal/scratch/` and `harness/__pycache__/` — the three ignored
directories a harness run writes — restored from copies taken before the
pass, with the modification times of the directories that hold them. A
fingerprint of the checkout — `HEAD`, `git status --porcelain --ignored`, and
every path, size and modification time under its ignored directories, 33 986
lines — was taken before the pass and after the restore, and **the two are
equal**. *(The first comparison differed in one line, `build/`'s own size —
36 864 bytes before and 32 768 after, because the restore re-created the
directory and a directory's size is its entry blocks; grown back with empty
entries, which were then removed, and its time reset, the line matched.)*
What the pass leaves is in `.git` alone: the objects step 6's `git add -A`
wrote, which nothing references, and the reflog's entry for the reset.
Tracked files hold the commit's contents; their modification times are the
reset's.

**Since the real pass, only this section has changed**, written after it; and
since pass 4, besides it, one sentence of prose in §6, which quotes the
dispatch. A digest of every marked and every `bash` block of this file, taken
of the text pass 4 ran, of the text the real pass ran and of this one: **258
blocks in each, 0 changed**. The commit was then amended with this section,
still unpushed.

**The measurements before the passes** — the lexical and token programs at
both pins and at the workbench's control copy `9f6f370`, the arm bills of
every running file at both pins, the slices over `cap`, the `sweep` stage's
timings and the `cstring` census — were taken in the planner's scratch copies,
never in the tree, and `facts.py` re-derived every one of them but the
control copy's in every pass. **Every leg ran, in the real position**:
`facts.py`, `lexsame.py` and `mut.py` find the kept toolchains and the sibling
through `$WB`, which `env.sh` resolves from `$REPO`, so nothing that reads a
checkout-relative path could have been skipped (the workbench PLAYBOOK §12).

## Execution record

*(Written by the worker. The first half appends its record and a REPORT with
`status: READY-TO-CLOSE`; the second half appends the triage, the archive and
a REPORT with `status: DONE`. A committed REPORT is never edited — W-28.)*
